# Open Issues — Living Finding Backlog

This is the single persistent backlog of Hardware Reviewer, Mechanical
Reviewer (Phase 1 — `docs/architecture-evolution.md` §31), and `rubber-duck`
findings across all review cycles — one shared backlog across disciplines,
not one per discipline. `validation/design-review.md` is the per-cycle
report; this file is what actually gates Design Complete
(`docs/architecture.md` §8) and what CI checks
(`.github/workflows/hardware-gate.yml` + `tools/check_open_issues.py`).

**Keep the table header and column order below exactly as-is** — the CI
gate script parses this file as a Markdown table.

## SLA Policy

*(Set by the human Chief Engineer per project — the framework does not
hard-code day counts.)*

| Severity | Target time to resolution |
|---|---|
| CRITICAL | SLA: [human sets] |
| HIGH | SLA: [human sets] |
| MEDIUM | SLA: [human sets] |
| LOW | SLA: [human sets] |

## Rules

- **CRITICAL** can only reach `RESOLVED` — never `ACCEPTED-RISK`
  (`docs/architecture.md` §8).
- **HIGH** may become `ACCEPTED-RISK` only with a named human Chief Engineer
  sign-off, written rationale, and date (recorded in the `Notes` column or
  cross-referenced to `validation/change-log.md`).
- **MEDIUM/LOW** don't block Design Complete but should still get a
  disposition (`RESOLVED` / `DEFERRED` / `ACCEPTED-RISK`) before archiving a
  revision.
- `Source` distinguishes which review lens found it: `hardware-reviewer`
  (Electronics checklist/failure-mode review), `mechanical-reviewer`
  (Mechanical checklist/failure-mode review, Phase 1 —
  `docs/architecture-evolution.md` §31), or `rubber-duck` (premise/
  assumption review) — see `docs/architecture.md` §5.1. Never merge/relabel
  one as another.

| ISS-002 | HIGH | ACCEPTED-RISK | LDO Vin ROC top-end margin against real-world USB-C vSafe5V ceiling is effectively zero | U3 (TLV75533PDBVR LDO); J1 (USB-C connector) | DS-PWR-002/003 | Operating above ROC (5.5V) but below AMR (6.0V) risks reduced functionality/reliability/lifetime per TI's own ROC-exceedance footnote; real USB-C vSafe5V ceiling (4.75-5.5V) is wider than REQ-101's stated 4.75-5.25V | Explicitly scope REQ-101 to intended sources, or add real circuit-level margin given bench-use (REQ-201) source diversity | hardware-reviewer | [human sets] | 2026-08-31 | 2026-09-01 | Cross-ref design-review.md ISS-002; CE's arithmetic against REQ-101 is correct, but REQ-101 itself may not capture true USB-C input range. **Cycle 2 (2026-09-01) independent assessment: technical analysis SOUND** — both governing numbers (vSafe5V=4.75–5.5V; LDO AMR=6.0V/ROC=1.45–5.5V) independently re-derived and confirmed to match Rev 2 §3.5's arithmetic exactly. The AMR-vs-ROC distinction is correctly characterized: this is a possible-reduced-functionality-at-a-worst-case-edge concern, **not a damage/destruction risk** (0.5V/9% of real headroom remains under the 6.0V AMR even at the worst-case 5.5V input). **ACCEPTED-RISK — human Chief Engineer sign-off recorded 2026-09-01** (via `ask_user`, session record): "Accept the risk as-is — no damage risk, bench-use context", selected over the alternative of routing to Component Engineer for an LDO with a wider Vin ROC ceiling. Rationale: this is a first-cycle, bench/prototype design (REQ-201 desk/lab use, REQ-502 paper-design exercise this cycle, no mass production), the failure mode is bounded (reduced functionality/reliability at a rare worst-case input, not device destruction), and re-selecting the approved LDO would reopen an already-approved Component Selection decision for a non-damage-risk edge case. If this design is ever mass-produced or its input-source population becomes less controlled, this disposition should be revisited. See `validation/change-log.md` ECO-003. |

| ISS-015 | HIGH | RESOLVED | DRV10983 (U5) SPEED pin's factory-default EEPROM state is active analog mode (not inert), has no hardware-enforced cross-domain power-up sequencing, and its cited partial mitigant (internal SPEED pulldown) is documented only for the sleep-capable Z-variant, not the plain DRV10983 actually specified -- uncommanded-motion risk on M1 tied to REQ-403 | U5 (DRV10983) SPEED pin 13 / DIR pin 14; M1 (T-Motor MN2206-13 KV2000 reaction wheel); J1 (MCU/USB domain) vs. J4 (independently-sourced motor domain) under the Option A dual-power architecture | DS-MTR-068 (qualified this cycle), DS-MTR-069, DS-MTR-070, DS-MTR-071 (new this cycle) -- DRV10983 Table 8 EEPROM defaults, §8.4.5.2 analog-mode transfer function, RPD_SPEED_SL's own "SLEEP MODE (DRV10983Z)" Electrical Characteristics section header | Register 0x2B (SysOpt9) factory-default value 0x0C decodes to SpdCtrlMd=0 = analog mode, not the PWM mode this design intends (which requires an EEPROM write firmware has not yet performed in any uncommissioned window); §8.4.5.2 confirms analog mode is fully active out of reset (SPEED<100mV=stop, 100mV<=SPEED<~V3P3x0.9=proportional non-zero speed command, SPEED>=~V3P3x0.9=max). No EN/FAULT pin exists on DRV10983 at all -- SPEED is the sole on/off control surface. No supervisory switch gates U5's VCC on the MCU domain's power state (D2/D3 are both passive) -- the uncommissioned window is not bounded to be transient if the motor-rail connector (J4) is powered before or independent of the MCU-rail connector (J1). Any leakage/coupling/stray voltage on floating SPEED exceeding 100mV during this window is interpreted as a real, non-zero commanded speed, commutating M1 on generic (non-T-Motor-matched) EEPROM Rm/Kt defaults | (1) Add a real external SPEED pulldown sized to dominate realistic leakage/coupling -- a deliberate, justified deviation from TI's own Table 11 reference circuit, which implicitly assumes a single always-alive host and does not anticipate this design's dual-independent-power-domain architecture; (2) add a supervisory load switch gating U5's VCC, enabled only once the MCU domain's own rail is confirmed alive, directly enforcing the missing cross-domain sequencing (design document's own §16 item 14); or (3), as a weaker documentation/process-only fallback, mandate a specific operator power-up order and have firmware drive SPEED to a safe state at the earliest possible init step. Given REQ-403's explicit human-review gate, route for human safety sign-off regardless of which technical path is chosen | hardware-reviewer | [human sets] | 2026-09-05 | 2026-09-08 | Independently discovered this cycle -- not one of the two items the design document itself flagged for Hardware Reviewer attention -- while investigating checklist items 6 (floating pin) and 10 (power sequencing) together, building on the design document's own honest self-flagged §16 items 13-14. This review's contribution beyond the design document's own analysis: (a) independently confirmed via Table 8's own EEPROM-default decode that the factory-default state is analog mode, not inert, correcting an inaccurate "functionally inert" claim in the pre-existing `datasheets/evidence-log.md` DS-MTR-068 entry (qualified this cycle -- see `validation/design-review.md` Cycle 3 for the correction detail, plus the new DS-MTR-071 row); (b) independently found that RPD_SPEED_SL -- the design document's own cited partial mitigant -- is documented under a datasheet section header explicitly scoped to the DRV10983Z sleep-capable variant, not confirmed/guaranteed for the plain (Standby-only) DRV10983 actually specified as U5, a variant-specificity nuance beyond the design document's own (already honest) sleep-mode-vs-power-on-state caveat. Severity HIGH not CRITICAL: the failure path is probabilistic/multi-factor-dependent (a specific power-up order plus an unmeasured real stray voltage exceeding 100mV), not deterministic-every-time, and any resulting motion is current-limited/ramped (StAccel/OpenLCurr, Table 6) and running on non-matched commutation parameters, bounding (not eliminating) worst-case outcome -- consistent with this file's own HIGH-not-CRITICAL precedent (e.g. ISS-011, a deterministic 100%-certain failure under stated conditions, still classified HIGH not CRITICAL). Cross-ref `validation/design-review.md` Cycle 3; ties directly to REQ-403 (safety-critical, human-review-gated) and checklist items 6/10/15. **Cycle 4 (2026-09-08) independent re-verification: CONFIRMED RESOLVED.** R10=1kΩ (unchanged since Rev 4) independently confirmed present. New U6 (TI TPS26631PWPR) load switch's R11=10kΩ SHDN pulldown independently re-derived from TI's own guaranteed ≥10µA SHDN-sink instruction (not the design document's restated figure) — reproduces the claimed ≈8×/≈20× margins against V(SHUTF)=0.8V/V(SHUTR)=2V exactly. Independently investigated this cycle's specific new question — PA9's own state before MCU GPIO init completes — via `web_search` (secondary source, disclosed in `validation/design-review.md` Cycle 4): confirmed STM32G0 GPIOs default to high-impedance Analog mode (no internal pull) after any reset (now formally logged as DS-MCU-067, MODERATE confidence), so R11 dominates the SHDN node with full margin for the entire pre-init boot window; PA9 driving SHDN high draws only ≈330µA through R11, confirming the enable direction also works correctly. No new floating-pin risk found on PA9. See `validation/design-review.md` Cycle 4 for the full derivation. |

| ISS-020 | HIGH | ACCEPTED-RISK | The approved "≥3000 RPM" reaction-wheel target has only ever been treated as a functional floor, never converted into a bounded maximum/safety envelope for commanded speed, acceleration, or mechanical containment | M1 (T-Motor MN2206-13 KV2000) and its eventual flywheel/mount (Mechanical Lead scope); U5 SPEED command path; motor firmware (future) | DS-MTR-018 (M1's own derived no-load speed is ≈20,000-22,200 RPM, 6-7× the 3000 RPM target); DS-MTR-020/DS-MTR-021 (torque/mass basis); REQ-007, REQ-306, REQ-403 | REQ-007 only specifies a commanded range "including at least" the target -- nothing in this design or its requirements bounds the maximum command, ramp rate, or overspeed response. For the 100g/30mm flywheel basis, stored rotational energy at the motor's own derived no-load speed is roughly 45-55x the energy at the 3000 RPM target (energy scales with omega^2) -- a legitimate full-duty command, a firmware bug, or a lost/reduced mechanical load could drive the wheel far beyond the value every other requirement/mechanical-design assumption in this cycle was sized against, with no interlock to catch it; this directly compounds ISS-015's uncommissioned-motion finding (that finding is about *unintended* motion at power-up, this finding is about *unbounded* motion even during normal, intended operation) | Define an explicit maximum operating/fault speed (not just the minimum target), a commanded ramp-rate limit, and a tach-supervised (FG-based) overspeed shutdown as a firmware safety interlock -- this is a bounded safety cutoff, not the prohibited closed-loop attitude-control logic REQ-009 excludes, the same way a supply's overcurrent shutdown is not "control" in the disallowed sense. Feed the chosen maximum into Mechanical Lead's flywheel/mount/containment design as a real input, not an afterthought | rubber-duck | [human sets] | 2026-09-05 | 2026-09-09 | A genuine premise-challenge finding -- neither the requirements pass, Component Selection, nor Circuit Design ever asked "what is the MAXIMUM speed this design must tolerate/survive," only "what is the minimum it must reach." Cross-ref `validation/design-review.md` Cycle 3 rubber-duck section. **Cycle 4 (2026-09-08) independent re-verification: correctly remains OPEN, not hardware-fixable.** Independently confirmed via direct read of new design-doc §7.5.11 that Circuit Engineer honestly disclosed "No circuit-level fix exists for this finding, and none is invented here," and independently confirmed REQ-405 exists in `requirements/requirements.md` (line 142), is well-formed (Priority: Must), and is correctly attributed to this finding — added by the Hardware Lead's own mediation commit (`9410a9d`), not silently invented by Circuit Engineer. My own independent assessment agrees no circuit-level mitigation exists for this firmware/control-policy question. Recommend a human Chief Engineer consider an explicit disposition note (e.g. "OPEN, tracked for Firmware Bring-up phase") for the record, though the underlying finding itself must remain OPEN (not ACCEPTED-RISK) until the firmware policy is actually implemented — per this file's own Rules, only a named human Chief Engineer may make an ACCEPTED-RISK disposition change, and that has not occurred this cycle. See `validation/design-review.md` Cycle 4. **ACCEPTED-RISK — human Chief Engineer sign-off recorded 2026-09-09** (via cross-session HITL channel, hybrid disposition covering ISS-020 and ISS-021 together): explicitly conditioned on Firmware Bring-up actually implementing REQ-405 (this finding's governing requirement) — this is not a bare accept-and-move-on, the underlying finding is not truly RESOLVED until that firmware exists and is independently confirmed against REQ-405 in a future review cycle. Rationale distinguishing this from Bench-IMU-01's own precedent (Firmware Bring-up not gating Design Complete): that precedent was safe specifically because Rev 1/2 firmware only *read* sensors and never drove physical motion; this subsystem has real stored rotational energy, so the same blanket precedent cannot be applied without a stronger compensating safeguard. That safeguard: the "before first power-on" HITL gate (`docs/architecture.md` §10) is strengthened with a new named, mandatory pre-condition in `validation/bring-up-procedure.md` requiring confirmed REQ-405 implementation, cross-referencing this exact finding ID, before the motor subsystem is ever energized — see that file's pre-power-on section. This lets hardware/mechanical Design Complete proceed now while keeping the actual physical risk hard-blocked at the one gate that matters. See `validation/change-log.md` for the corresponding ECO entry. |
| ISS-021 | HIGH | ACCEPTED-RISK | REQ-404's "shutdown behavior to prevent sustained overheating" is not actually satisfied by DRV10983's Lock Detection alone -- Lock Detection auto-retries after a fixed delay, it does not latch off | U5 (DRV10983); M1; D2 (thermal dissipation under repeated fault current) | DS-MTR-058 (OCP clears once the fault condition itself clears); DS-MTR-059 (Lock Detection auto-retries after tLOCK_OFF=5s, not a latch); DS-MTR-060 (Thermal Shutdown recovers after cooling); REQ-404 | All three of U5's own protection mechanisms (OCP, Lock Detection, Thermal Shutdown) are auto-recovering/auto-retrying by design, not latching -- none of them, alone or together, actually implements "prevent sustained overheating" in the sense of a shutdown that stays off until an operator acknowledges it. A persistent jam (e.g. a mechanical bind on the flywheel) would cause repeated current pulses and repeated restart attempts indefinitely, each one dissipating real heat in U5, M1, and D2 (D2 alone can dissipate approximately 1.86W at a 3A/0.62V worst-case fault current), with no design-level cutoff that ever actually stops trying | Define an explicit latched-fault policy: e.g., firmware counts consecutive Lock Detection retries within a time window and forces SPEED to a safe/stopped state (and/or a supervisory switch cuts U5's VCC) after a threshold, requiring a deliberate re-arm before resuming -- do not treat "the driver retries automatically" as equivalent to "the system shuts down," those are different behaviors and REQ-404 asks for the latter | rubber-duck | [human sets] | 2026-09-05 | 2026-09-09 | Directly challenges whether REQ-404 (as currently implemented) is actually satisfied by U5's own datasheet-confirmed mechanisms, rather than checking whether those mechanisms exist (which Hardware Reviewer already confirmed via DS-MTR-058/059/060) -- a premise-level distinction between "protection exists" and "the specific behavior REQ-404 asks for exists." Cross-ref `validation/design-review.md` Cycle 3 rubber-duck section. **Cycle 4 (2026-09-08) independent re-verification: correctly remains OPEN (firmware policy undecided), hardware enabler independently CONFIRMED sufficient.** Independently confirmed via direct read of new design-doc §7.5.12 the same honest "no circuit-level fix" disclosure, and independently confirmed REQ-406 exists (line 143, Priority: Should), correctly attributed. Independently verified the one genuinely hardware-level sub-question this finding raises — whether U6/PA9 provides a real, sufficient physical enforcement point for a future firmware latch — and confirmed PA9 driving U6's SHDN low removes power from U5 entirely (not merely a SPEED-level override), the strongest available physical enforcement mechanism at this architecture; also independently confirmed via TI's own Table 8-1 that U6's own MODE=Open gives an independent chip-level Latch-off (TPS26631-specific), sharing but not confusing the SHDN reset line with any future firmware-declared latch (design doc's own §16 item 25 accurately discloses that U6 exposes no register letting firmware distinguish the two). Same recommendation as ISS-020: ripe for human Chief Engineer disposition consideration; remains OPEN, not unilaterally changed here. See `validation/design-review.md` Cycle 4. **ACCEPTED-RISK — human Chief Engineer sign-off recorded 2026-09-09** (via cross-session HITL channel, same hybrid disposition covering ISS-020 and ISS-021 together — see ISS-020's Notes for the full rationale): explicitly conditioned on Firmware Bring-up actually implementing REQ-406 (this finding's governing requirement) — the underlying finding is not truly RESOLVED until that firmware exists and is independently confirmed against REQ-406 in a future review cycle. | ISS-026 | CRITICAL | RESOLVED | The IMU (U2, BMI270) I2C bus, documented and firmware-implemented as "I2C2 on PB10/PB11" since the original Rev 2 baseline, is wired to two MCU pins that **do not physically exist** on the STM32G031K8T6's actual LQFP-32 package | U1 (STM32G031K8T6) PB10/PB11 nets; U2 (BMI270) SCx/SDx pins; R3/R4 (I2C pull-ups); `firmware/bench-imu-01/src/{gpio.c,gpio.h,clock.c,i2c2.h,stm32g031_regs.h}` | DS12992 Rev 4 (ST's own primary datasheet, independently re-fetched and read directly this session via `r.jina.ai` text extraction — the same primary document DS-MCU-050 through DS-MCU-053/062 already cite): **Table 2** ("device features and peripheral counts") lists only 30 GPIOs for the K-suffix (LQFP32/UFQFPN32) package vs. 44 for the C-suffix (LQFP48/UFQFPN48) package; **Figure 7** ("STM32G031KxT LQFP32 pinout") lists all 32 physical pads by name and PB10/PB11 do not appear anywhere in it, while **Figure 9** ("STM32G031CxT LQFP48 pinout") explicitly does show them; **Table 12** ("Pin assignment and description," the master per-pin-per-package table) shows PB10's and PB11's own rows with a dash (not present) in the LQFP32/UFQFPN32 column and a real pin number (22, 23 respectively) only in the LQFP48/UFQFPN48 column. Independently cross-corroborated via a second, differently-sourced primary-equivalent record: ST's own CubeMX pin database as consumed by `stm32duino/Arduino_Core_STM32`'s auto-generated `PeripheralPins.c` for this exact part/package (`variants/STM32G0xx/G031K(4-6-8)(T-U)_G041K(6-8)(T-U)/PeripheralPins.c`) — PB10/PB11 appear in zero of its ADC/I2C/TIM/UART/SPI pin-map tables. New Evidence ID **DS-MCU-073** (logged as "DS-MCU-068" when this finding was first recorded 2026-09-09; renumbered 2026-09-11 during the main-branch merge, ECO-014) records this in full | The design as documented and as implemented in firmware is 100% guaranteed to fail at first power-on in exactly the way it is specified to work — this is not a corner-case/realistic-conditions failure (which would be HIGH), it is unconditional failure under the design's own normal/as-designed operating condition (`docs/architecture.md` §7.1's CRITICAL definition, first clause: "design will fail... under normal/expected operating conditions as designed"), because the two GPIO pads the schematic, net list, and firmware register-level code all reference for the IMU's SCL/SDA signals are simply not bonded out of the package — there is no physical pin for the PCB trace to connect to. The MCU's I2C2 peripheral would be correctly clocked and configured by firmware, but its AF6-routed output would have no physical pin to appear on; the IMU would never receive a valid I2C transaction, `BMI270_INIT_FAILED` would be the only possible outcome of every physical bring-up attempt, and REQ-002 (IMU functional readout, this board's core purpose) would be unconditionally unmet | Re-route the IMU I2C bus from the nonexistent PB10(SCL)/PB11(SDA) to **PA11(SCL)/PA12(SDA)** — both independently confirmed present on this exact LQFP32 package (Table 12), both carrying the same **I2C2** peripheral (not a peripheral-instance change, unlike the alternative of moving to I2C1) via the same **AF6** alternate-function selector already assumed for PB10/PB11 (Table 12: "SPI1_MISO/I2S1_MCK, USART1_CTS, TIM1_CH4, TIM1_BK2, **I2C2_SCL**" for PA11; "SPI1_MOSI/I2S1_SD, USART1_RTS_DE_CK, TIM1_ETR, I2S_CKIN, **I2C2_SDA**" for PA12), and both confirmed currently free/unused anywhere in this design through Rev 5 (`bench-imu-01-design.md` §11's own free-GPIO inventory) — no conflict with the motor subsystem's PA6/PA8/PA9/PB1/PB6/PB7 allocations. (The alternative of moving to real I2C1 pins is not available: PB6/PB7, I2C1's primary AF pair, are already consumed by the Rev 3 motor subsystem's own separate I2C1 bus to U5; PB8/PB9, I2C1's secondary AF pair, would require both an unavailable second simultaneous use of the same already-committed I2C1 hardware peripheral and a full firmware peripheral-instance change, a strictly larger blast radius than the PA11/PA12 fix.) Requires: (a) Circuit Engineer corrects the schematic net list, pin table, parts-list notes, and evidence-log citations; (b) Firmware Engineer corrects the register-level GPIO/AF/clock configuration (`gpio.c`/`gpio.h`/`clock.c` move from GPIOB pins 10/11 to GPIOA pins 11/12; `i2c2.h`/`stm32g031_regs.h` keep the I2C2 peripheral base address, which is unaffected by this fix since the peripheral instance itself does not change) — deferred to the already-planned Firmware Bring-up phase for this revision rather than a separate dispatch, tracked explicitly so it is not forgotten | human | CRITICAL: [human sets] | 2026-09-09 | 2026-09-10 | Discovered by a parallel session building a real KiCad project for the Rev 2 electronics baseline, relayed to this session by the human Chief Engineer with an explicit instruction to independently re-verify before acting. Independently re-verified beyond the human's own claim: fetched and read ST's actual primary datasheet (DS12992 Rev 4) directly this session (not merely trusting the relay or a secondary AI-summarized web search, two of which initially gave *conflicting* answers before this direct primary-source check settled it definitively), and independently cross-corroborated via a second, differently-derived primary-equivalent source (stm32duino's CubeMX-derived pin tables). This is a genuine, previously-undetected defect in the already-merged, already-Design-Complete Rev 2 baseline (Electronics PR #6, `b7ee9f1`) — it predates and is unrelated to Rev 3's own motor-subsystem work, but blocks Rev 3's own Design Complete Gate regardless, since CRITICAL findings can never be ACCEPTED-RISK (`docs/architecture.md` §8) and this finding is discovered while Rev 3 is the current revision under active review. The original Rev 2 Independent Review (which correctly caught the I2C1-vs-I2C2 *peripheral*-identity mislabeling, ISS-011/FMEA-003) did not separately check whether the specific GPIO pads referenced physically exist on the specific ordered package — a distinct, more fundamental question that FMEA-003's own "systemic lesson" (continue running both hardware-reviewer and rubber-duck passes) did not, in hindsight, fully cover either, since neither review lens's checklist explicitly included "does this named pin physically exist on this exact package variant." Recommend a new systemic-lesson entry in `validation/fmea.md` alongside FMEA-003 when the Rev 3 FMEA closeout pass runs. No mechanical-design impact: this is a pure MCU-internal net reassignment (which internal pad the IMU's SCL/SDA traces terminate at), not a change to board outline, connector positions, physical component list, or anything `hardware/mechanical-interface.md`/the Rev 3 enclosure design already produced — those remain valid and do not need rework. **Cycle 5 (2026-09-10) independent re-verification: CONFIRMED RESOLVED.** Independently re-fetched ST's own primary datasheet (DS12992 Rev 4) fresh this cycle via `r.jina.ai` — not reused from the citation chain — and re-derived the core physical fact from scratch: Table 2's GPIO/pin-count row (30 GPIOs, K-suffix/LQFP32-UFQFPN32 package vs. 44, C-suffix/LQFP48-UFQFPN48); Figure 7's full 32-pad LQFP32 pinout enumeration (PB10/PB11 absent, PA11/PA12 present); Figure 9's 48-pad LQFP48 enumeration (PB10/PB11/PB12 present); and Table 12's per-pin per-package assignment table, whose six-package-column parsing (SO8N/WLCSP18/TSSOP20/UFQFPN28/LQFP32-UFQFPN32/LQFP48-UFQFPN48 pin-position values) was independently validated against a known-good row (PC13: dash×5 then "1" = LQFP48-only, physical pin 1) before trusting it for the pins under review — reproduces the design document's own claim exactly: PB10 = dash×5 (incl. the LQFP32/UFQFPN32 column) then **22** only in the LQFP48/UFQFPN48 column; PB11 = same dash pattern then **23**; PA11 = 5/C1/16/18/**22**/dash (physical pin 22 present on LQFP32/UFQFPN32 itself); PA12 = 6/B2/17/19/**23**/dash (physical pin 23). Table 13 (Port A AF map) independently confirms PA11 AF6=I2C2_SCL and PA12 AF6=I2C2_SDA; Table 14 (Port B AF map) independently confirms PB10 AF6=I2C2_SCL and PB11 AF6=I2C2_SDA — the identical AF6 selector, exactly as the fix claims. **Completeness**: a fresh combined `grep -n "PB10"`/`"PB11"` pass (43 hits, whole document) reviewed line-by-line — every remaining hit is Rev 6/Rev 2 changelog narrative or a live table/prose entry that correctly states PA11/PA12 as the current value with PB10/PB11 present only as historical/superseded annotation (§2.3, §5.3, §5.5/§6/§7.5.4, §11's table + Rev 6 note, §12 net list, §14/§16 self-check items) — no live incorrect claim found anywhere. **Conflict check**: a fresh `grep -n "PA11"`/`"PA12"` (27 hits each) confirms sole use is the IMU's I2C2 SCL/SDA in every occurrence; cross-checked against the motor subsystem's own PA6/PA8/PA9/PB1/PB6/PB7 allocations (§7.5.4) — no overlap. Free-GPIO arithmetic independently re-added (§11: 11 enumerated free pins + PB8/PB9 = 13, down from 15 with PA11/PA12 now committed) — consistent. **§5.2 pull-up sizing independently re-read**: `Rp,min=(Vcc−VOL,max)/IOL` and `tr≈0.8473×Rp×Cb` depend only on Vcc/VOL,max/IOL/Cb, none of which reference a physical pin — and, going beyond the design document's own stated reasoning, Table 12 shows PB10/PB11 and PA11/PA12 (LQFP32/UFQFPN32 row) share the **identical I/O structure designation "FT_fa"**, which is what actually sets VOL,max/IOL in ST's electrical-characteristics tables — independently confirming R3/R4's 4.7kΩ sizing is unaffected, not merely assumed to be. **Floating pin / pull-up-pull-down / logic-voltage / interface-timing** (the four checklist items independently re-derived as relevant to a pin-reassignment specifically): no floating pin (R3/R4 pull-ups travel with the net; PB10/PB11 removed from the pin table entirely rather than left as a dangling used-but-unconnected entry); pull-up/pull-down correct (per the FT_fa-identity finding above); no logic-voltage mismatch (Port A and Port B share the same single VDD/VDDIO 3.3V I/O domain on this MCU — confirmed via the identical FT_fa I/O-structure class, not port letter, governing electrical behavior); no interface-timing impact (same I2C2 peripheral instance, same AF6 selector — alternate-function pad routing does not alter the peripheral's internal Fast-mode 400kHz timing-register configuration). **Scope discipline** independently confirmed via `git show --stat`/`--name-status ef5edc4` (not merely the commit message's own self-report): exactly one file touched, `hardware/schematic/bench-imu-01-design.md` (201 insertions/29 deletions) — `firmware/bench-imu-01/`, `bom/component-selection.md`, `requirements/requirements.md`, `hardware/mechanical-interface.md`, and `hardware/mechanical/` all confirmed untouched; `git status` clean, HEAD=`ef5edc4`, no further drift. | MISS-008 | CRITICAL | RESOLVED | Flywheel disk Z-stack formula omits hub-collar height, producing an empirically-confirmed ~145-151mm³ solid-solid interpenetration between the disk and its own mounting collar — the flywheel cannot physically be assembled as the design's own assembly sequence describes | Flywheel disk (`fw_disk_bottom`/`fw_disk_top`); hub collar (`fw_hub_collar_od`/`fw_hub_collar_h`); every parameter downstream of `fw_disk_top` (`fw_clearance_top`, containment wall height, REQ-306 vertical-clearance claim, REQ-403 containment envelope sizing) in bench-imu-01-enclosure.scad | bench-imu-01-enclosure.scad lines 384-392 (`fw_hub_standoff`=3.0mm DERIVED, `fw_hub_collar_h`=6.0mm ASSUMPTION, and the file's own `fw_shaft_exposed_len_needed = fw_hub_collar_h + fw_hub_standoff` = 9.0mm formula, which additively includes collar height) cross-referenced against lines 561-574 (`fw_disk_bottom = fw_motor_bell_top + fw_hub_standoff`, which omits `fw_hub_collar_h` entirely); bench-imu-01-dimensional-spec.md §14 Assembly Step 5 ("slide the flywheel disk onto the hub collar") and §7 (REQ-306 vertical clearance claim, which depends on the now-shown-incorrect fw_disk_top/fw_clearance_top values) | The design's own `fw_shaft_exposed_len_needed` formula (line 392) treats the collar as sitting above the standoff and the disk as sitting above the collar (additive: standoff + collar), yet `fw_disk_bottom` (line 565) positions the disk directly on top of the standoff, skipping the collar's own 6.0mm height entirely — an internal self-contradiction within the same file. Empirically confirmed via a standalone rendered `intersection()` of the two literal solids at their modeled coordinates (collar Z=[28.5,34.5], disk Z=[31.5,36.0]): a genuine, fully-manifold overlap solid, volume ~145.0mm³ via trimesh (hand-calc 150.8mm³, within normal STL-facet-discretization tolerance), Z-bounds exactly [31.5,34.5] — the bottom 3.0mm of the disk's modeled position is literally inside the collar's modeled volume. A "collar embedded within disk" alternative reading is independently ruled out: collar height (6.0mm) exceeds disk thickness (4.5mm), making that reading geometrically impossible. As modeled, and as the assembly sequence's own wording requires (disk slides onto/over the collar's full height before resting on top), the disk cannot occupy the position the file assigns it | Correct `fw_disk_bottom` to `fw_motor_bell_top + fw_hub_standoff + fw_hub_collar_h` (=37.5mm, not 31.5mm) and propagate through `fw_disk_top` (=42.0mm, not 36.0mm) and `fw_clearance_top` (=45.0mm, not 39.0mm). Independently re-derived `fw_disk_bottom`=37.5 / `fw_disk_top`=42.0 / `fw_clearance_top`=45.0 / `fw_cap_outer_top`=49.0mm directly from the live `.scad` source via `include` (not transcribed from the spec document), then independently confirmed zero overlap two ways: an isolated CGAL `intersection()` of the corrected disk and hub-collar solids renders empty, and a `union()` of the same two solids splits into 2 disjoint watertight components with an exact measured 3.0mm gap. | MISS-011 | MEDIUM | RESOLVED | REQ-403 containment disposition's specific wall-thickness (4mm) and fastener-retention adequacy claims rest on qualitative/heuristic reasoning, not any impact-energy or pull-out-under-shock calculation tied to the disposition's own stated ~122J/100g hazard figure | Containment wall (`containment_wall_t`=4.0mm) and 6xM3 heat-set-insert cap fasteners (bench-imu-01-enclosure.scad); REQ-403 safety disposition (bench-imu-01-dimensional-spec.md §8) | bench-imu-01-dimensional-spec.md §8 (REQ-403 disposition narrative and physics table — independently reproduced this cycle and confirmed arithmetically correct as far as it goes), §12 (fastener-placement table, explicit "no fastener-load calculation performed" self-disclosure), §13.3 (explicit self-disclosed lack of print-orientation-vs-impact-load analysis for the cap) | The disposition's reasoning for why a 4mm wall (=2x the design's own general-purpose print-safety minimum) and 6xM3 heat-set inserts are adequate specifically against a detached-flywheel impact is stated qualitatively ("substantially thicker than structural minimum," "multiple fasteners provide redundancy") rather than derived from the disposition's own stated ~122J/100g kinetic-energy hazard figure via any impact-energy-absorption, wall-deflection-under-impact, or fastener pull-out-under-shock-load calculation — honestly self-disclosed 3 times (§8/§12/§13.3) rather than silently asserted; Non-gating (MEDIUM) per `docs/architecture.md` §7.1: a disclosed rigor/traceability gap on a proposal still pending human review, not an independently-demonstrated physical defect — no confirmed malfunction or hazard has been shown, only an absence of the calculation that would rule one out. Must still be explicitly surfaced to the human REQ-403 safety-review gate rather than silently dropped. Distinguished from MISS-008 (CRITICAL, an independently-proven geometric defect) — this reviewer's assessment is that the REQ-403 disposition should not be presented to the human gate until MISS-008 is fixed and the containment envelope is re-verified against corrected geometry, since a containment proposal built on a Z-stack that cannot physically hold the flywheel in the position the design assumes is not yet a stable target for a rigor-gap assessment either. **Rev 3.2 update (Mechanical Lead, 2026-09-13):** attempted Recommended-Fix path (a) — a bounded, closed-form wall-impact/penetration estimate (two independent methods: Charpy-toughness energy-density scaling, and yield-strength-limited local plastic work; both swept across a physically-anchored engagement-length range, disk thickness 4.5mm to disk diameter 60mm) and a fastener pull-out-vs-load estimate (real published/measured M3 heat-set-insert pull-out data — CNC Kitchen direct-read primary source, Sculpteo secondary corroboration — compared against a work-energy force estimate for the disclosed load case), full detail at `bench-imu-01-dimensional-spec.md` §8.1 (Rev 3.2). 6 new Evidence IDs registered (DS-FAST-001/002/003, DS-MTL-001/002/003; see `datasheets/evidence-log.md` and their individual metadata records under `datasheets/`). Every number confidence-marked CONFIRMED/ASSUMPTION/ESTIMATE/UNKNOWN per `.github/instructions/mechanical-design.instructions.md`. Result: the wall estimate does NOT support an affirmative "4.0mm is adequate" claim — local-material energy absorption falls short of the full 121.60J budget by roughly 2.5-3x in the most generous physically-plausible case (yield-strength method, largest engagement length) and by one to over three orders of magnitude under more typical/localized engagement assumptions or when using fracture-toughness reasoning alone. This does not prove the wall fails outright (no FEA/physical-test tool exists in this environment to go further, and at least one identified simplification — treating the impact as square-on rather than the true oblique release geometry for a small-fragment sub-case — is conservative in the design's favor) but it does not clear the gap either; this has been separately escalated to the Hardware Lead (§8.1.6 of the same document) as a new, distinct finding, and no `.scad` geometry was changed in response. The fastener estimate is more favorable: plausible for the joint's realistic/secondary loading role (per the established primary-wall/secondary-cap load-path framing already in `bench-imu-01-manufacturing-spec.md`), and only marginal-to-inadequate for a hypothetical, pessimistic full-direct-hit scenario the cap was never intended to be the primary defense against. This closure attempt is proposed as READY for the Mechanical Reviewer's independent cross-check; per this project's convention that no discipline self-closes a reviewer-sourced finding, Status is intentionally left OPEN below, not marked RESOLVED, by this Mechanical Lead. **RESOLVED (Mechanical Reviewer, 2026-09-13), independent cross-check:** the Rev 3.2 closure attempt is confirmed genuine, defensible engineering work — every arithmetic step in `bench-imu-01-dimensional-spec.md` §8.1 was independently re-derived from scratch (not re-read) and matches exactly; all 6 new Evidence IDs (DS-FAST-001/002/003, DS-MTL-001/002/003) were independently re-fetched/re-searched against live/primary sources and confirmed accurately transcribed (one immaterial ~1MPa Prusament tensile-range transcription imprecision noted, does not affect any conclusion); the two-method local-absorption bounding approach was independently assessed as a reasonable, legitimate first-pass engineering bound given no FEA/physical-test tool exists in this environment, with no missing favorable global-structural argument found (see `validation/design-review.md`, "Mechanical Reviewer — MISS-011 Closure Attempt Independent Cross-Check + Wall-Margin Escalation Assessment," 2026-09-13, for full detail). MISS-011 was always about the *absence* of a calculation tying the wall/fastener adequacy claims to the disclosed hazard figure, not about achieving a reassuring result — Recommended Fix path (a) is now satisfied in full, even though the calculation's own result is concerning. That concerning result is not swept under this closure: it is tracked as its own new, distinct finding, **MISS-016 (HIGH)**, exactly mirroring how MISS-012 closed on its own specific bar while newly-discovered substantive concerns were spun off as MISS-013/014 rather than kept under MISS-012 itself. A separate, independently-discovered rigor gap (systemic confidence-mislabeling in §8.1's ledger) is tracked as **MISS-017 (MEDIUM)**. Scope discipline independently verified via `git diff`/`git status`: no `.scad` geometry changed, no `open-issues.md` row self-created or self-closed by the Mechanical Lead. |
| MISS-012 | HIGH | RESOLVED | REQ-403 containment disposition's `containment_wall_t`=4.0mm claim is a **CAD-geometric solid-wall claim only** — whether the physically 3D-printed part actually contains 4.0mm of solid material at that location, or a sparse internal lattice (typical FDM slicer defaults are 15-25% infill, mostly air), depends entirely on manufacturing/print-process parameters (infill %, infill pattern, wall/perimeter loop count, print orientation relative to expected impact-load direction) that are specified downstream of CAD, at slicing time — and are **never mentioned anywhere in this repository** | Containment wall/cap (`containment_wall_t`=4.0mm, `bench-imu-01-enclosure.scad`); REQ-403 safety disposition (`bench-imu-01-dimensional-spec.md` §8, §12) | `hardware/mechanical/bench-imu-01-dimensional-spec.md` line 372 (`containment_wall_t` row: "Deliberately thicker than the print-safe minimum -- this wall's job is containment (§8), not just enclosure, so it is sized above the manufacturability floor **on purpose**" -- reasoning is entirely about nominal solid-geometry wall thickness, with no reference to infill/perimeter/print-orientation anywhere); independently confirmed via a repo-wide `grep -rn -i "infill"` returning zero hits anywhere in the repository | For a part whose entire stated purpose is impact/fragment containment (not merely enclosure fit/dust-proofing, where a lower infill would be a normal, harmless cost/weight optimization), a solid-wall CAD dimension is not itself a manufacturing instruction -- if this part were sliced with a typical/default FDM setting (15-25% sparse infill, commonly a slicer's own out-of-the-box default) without anyone deliberately overriding it toward a containment-appropriate value, the as-printed wall would be mostly air at the exact location the design's entire safety argument depends on being solid, silently invalidating the ~15.9x-yield-safety-factor and containment-topology reasoning in §8 without changing a single number in the CAD file or this repository's own documentation -- a realistic, not remote-corner-case, failure mode given infill is a real parameter every FDM print requires *some* value for, and the repository-wide silence on it means the default would apply unless a human operator happens to know to override it | Do not attempt an ad hoc fix to this finding directly (no reasoned infill%/perimeter-count/print-orientation spec exists yet, and inventing one without a reviewed process would repeat the exact "improvised, ungoverned parameter" problem this finding is about). Correct path: this project is introducing a new **Manufacturing Engineer** discipline (agent + skill + a corresponding addition to Mechanical Reviewer's own checklist requiring a process spec for safety-critical/structural parts) as a standalone framework PR against `main`, mirroring the Power Engineer precedent (framework merged and audited before being exercised on a real design). Once that framework PR lands, get a Manufacturing Engineer pass on this containment cap specifically (infill %, wall/perimeter count, print orientation, each reasoned against the disposition's own disclosed ~99-122J credible-worst-case load), independently cross-checked by Mechanical Reviewer per the new checklist item, **before** REQ-403 sign-off is considered ready -- this is an additional prerequisite on top of, not instead of, the human Chief Engineer's own separate safety sign-off | human | HIGH: [human sets] | 2026-09-11 | 2026-09-11 | Surfaced by the human Chief Engineer's own direct question while reviewing the containment-cap proposal (relayed via the cross-session HITL channel) -- neither Mechanical Lead's own self-check, nor 2 Independent Mechanical Review cycles, caught this; a genuine premise-challenge finding in the same spirit as this project's own rubber-duck lens, though sourced directly from the human this time, not an AI review pass. Classified HIGH, not CRITICAL: this is not a demonstrated-certain failure of the current, complete design as documented (the "design" is genuinely incomplete on this specific parameter, not wrong), but a realistic-corner-case-that-is-actually-the-likely-default risk if the gap is left unaddressed through to physical manufacture -- distinguished from MISS-011 (MEDIUM, a rigor gap on an already-fully-specified geometry) because this finding is more fundamental: it questions whether the CAD geometry itself will correspond to the as-built part at all, not merely whether an already-agreed geometry has been load-verified. A separate, standalone framework PR (Manufacturing Engineer discipline) is already in motion against `main`, not yet merged or audited -- explicitly NOT to be improvised around ad hoc; this finding stays OPEN, tracked, and blocking a *complete* REQ-403 sign-off (though not blocking the human's own topology/general-approach review, which may still proceed) until that framework lands and is applied. **UPDATE 2026-09-11**: the Manufacturing Engineer framework has now landed and produced its first real output, `hardware/mechanical/bench-imu-01-manufacturing-spec.md`; Mechanical Reviewer has independently cross-checked it per the new checklist item 11 (see `validation/design-review.md`, "Mechanical Reviewer — Manufacturing Process Cross-Check (Containment Cap)"). Real, substantive progress, but **this finding remains OPEN, not RESOLVED**: the produced spec covers `containment_cap()` only; it does not specify any process for `fw_bay_wall()` (part of `module base()`), which shares this exact `containment_wall_t` claim and is independently confirmed (direct `.scad` geometry re-derivation) to be the actual primary radial containment surface in the disclosed threat's path -- the cap's own skirt sits ~9-11mm further out, radially behind the flange band. See new finding MISS-013 (HIGH) for the scope-gap specifics -- this MISS-012 finding should be considered resolved only once a process spec exists (and is independently cross-checked) for `fw_bay_wall()`/`base()` as well, not just the cap. This meets MISS-012's own stated closure bar verbatim: "a process spec exists (and is independently cross-checked) for `fw_bay_wall()`/`base()` as well, not just the cap." **RESOLVED** on that independent basis. Every remaining "121.60J"/"69.74 m/s" reference in the document is now an explicitly-labeled historical "(was ...)" comparison, not a live citation (repo-wide grep confirms this). Severity remains HIGH (unaffected in kind, only in magnitude). This finding, and its Rev 3.4 figures, were presented to the human Chief Engineer as a first-class part of the REQ-403 decision, not a footnote. **ACCEPTED-RISK — human Chief Engineer sign-off recorded 2026-09-13** (received directly from Kyosuke, relayed via the cross-session HITL channel — see `validation/change-log.md` ECO-024 for the corresponding entry): the containment structure (continuous 4.0mm `fw_bay_wall()` + bolted `containment_cap()`) is approved as a **defense-in-depth mitigation** — explicitly on the basis that "something is better than nothing," **not** on a claim that the calculated shortfall has been resolved or that containment adequacy has been proven. The corrected Rev 3.4 load case (156.44J/79.11 m/s) and MISS-016's own quantified shortfall (≈3.26–4.30× best case to ≈1.7–3.6 orders of magnitude typical) were both explicitly weighed as part of this decision, not overridden or disputed. **Real physical drop/impact destructive testing is explicitly deferred as a tracked future task, not resolved by this disposition** — see new finding **MISS-022** (LOW, Source: human) for the standalone tracked item, so this is not silently folded into "resolved." This finding is not truly certified/validated in the sense Manufacturing Engineer's own escalation used that word — it is accepted as the best currently-achievable engineering mitigation under this project's real tooling constraints (no physical printer, no destructive-test capability, no FEA), with the residual uncertainty knowingly carried forward by the human's own explicit decision, not by AI self-approval. |

U6, and therefore the entire motor + reaction wheel subsystem, can never be enabled by firmware under any condition | U1 (STM32G031K8T6) pin 19/PA9; U6 (TPS26631PWPR) pin 13/SHDN; R11 (10k pulldown); downstream: U5 (DRV10983), M1 (reaction wheel motor) -- the entire VM_MOTOR-gated subsystem | DS-MCU-064 (ST CubeMX XML pin database: pin 19 = PA9 by default, NC only if the unused PINREMAP silicon feature is engaged -- independently confirms PA9 is the objectively correct default identity for this pin, ruling out a repeat of the ISS-026/027 nonexistent-pin defect class); DS-PROT-024 (TPS26631 SHDN pin native bias: internal pull-up, floating/native state = device ON, per Section 8.3.13/Table 5-1); primary tool artifact independently inspected this cycle (not a manufacturer datasheet, disclosed as such): KiCad's own bundled `MCU_ST_STM32G0.kicad_sym` library file at `/Applications/KiCad/KiCad.app/Contents/SharedSupport/symbols/`, whose base symbol `STM32G031K_4-6-8_Tx` (extended by the placed `STM32G031K8Tx` symbol) declares pin 19 as electrical type `no_connect` (hidden), name "NC/PA9", with PA9 listed only as an alternate function | `generate_schematic.py` line 755 draws a wire+label at U1 pin 19 intending to net it to `U6_EN`, but pin 19's KiCad library base electrical type is `no_connect`, and the script's `kiutils` dependency has no support for KiCad's real per-instance "alternate pin function assignment" mechanism needed to actually activate the PA9 alternate for netlisting purposes -- confirmed the script never attempts this (0 occurrences of "alternate" in `generate_schematic.py`), and confirmed the 187 "alternate" occurrences already present in the committed `.kicad_sch` are all library-definition-level entries inside the `lib_symbols` cache block (ending by line 3135, well before the first placed symbol instance at line 8909) -- none are per-instance activations. The wire is drawn but KiCad's netlist compiler does not recognize it as a real connection: the compiled netlist places U1 pin 19 on a synthetic `unconnected-(U1-NC/PA9-Pad19)` net, and `/U6_EN` contains only {R11 pin 1, U6 pin 13}. Since R11 is a 10k-ohm pulldown deliberately sized to force U6's SHDN default-OFF pending an active-HIGH MCU override that this missing connection was supposed to provide, SHDN sits permanently near 0V: U6 never enables, VM_MOTOR never appears at U6 OUT, U5 never receives power, and M1 never spins, regardless of any SPEED/PWM command firmware issues. This unconditionally defeats REQ-007 (open-loop PWM speed control of the reaction wheel, this revision's headline new capability) under fully normal, expected operation -- not a corner-case or probabilistic risk, and directly analogous to the ISS-026/027 precedent's own CRITICAL classification for a materially identical failure class ("documented MCU connection does not actually reach its destination") | Ensure U1 physical pin 19 is a genuine member of `/U6_EN` in the compiled netlist -- e.g. by patching the schematic-generation tooling to emit KiCad's real per-instance `(pin "19" (alternate "PA9"))` assignment, or by opening the generated schematic in the actual KiCad GUI and using its interactive "Edit Pin Alternate Function" feature on this one pin before re-saving. Either path MUST be followed by a fresh, independent `kicad-cli sch export netlist` and `kicad-cli sch erc` re-run to CONFIRM pin 19 now appears as a member of `/U6_EN` and the `no_connect_connected` ERC warning is gone -- a visually-correct wire in the GUI schematic view is not sufficient evidence, as this finding itself demonstrates | hardware-reviewer | [human sets] | 2026-09-14 | 2026-09-02 | Independently triple-corroborated, not a single-method inference: (1) regenerated the schematic from the CURRENT script in a fresh, isolated `kiutils` venv (`/tmp/hwreview/regen/`) and independently re-exported ITS netlist -- identical result, ruling out a stale-committed-artifact explanation; (2) independently re-ran ERC directly (`kicad-cli sch erc`) and confirmed its own built-in `no_connect_connected` rule flags this exact pin+wire (raw message: "a pin of type 'unconnected' is connected", Symbol U1 pin 19 [NC/PA9]) -- originally logged by the schematic README as a benign cosmetic warning, now understood as a third independent confirmation angle of this same defect, not a separate/lesser issue; (3) directly read KiCad's own standard library symbol file to confirm the root mechanism. Important mitigating context, stated plainly and not omitted: this defect fails in the SAFE direction (U6 permanently OFF, not permanently ON) -- fully consistent with, and does not contradict, REQ-403's fail-safe/no-uncommanded-motion intent; there is no overvoltage/hazard risk, only a complete, deterministic non-function of the new subsystem. Also independently confirmed this is a design-doc-vs-KiCad-transcription defect specifically, not a flaw in the original architecture: Cycle 4 (2026-09-08, see ISS-021's own Notes in this file) already independently verified "PA9 driving U6's SHDN low removes power from U5 entirely" as sound at the design-document level -- this session's KiCad transcription (the first ever performed for this board) is what silently broke that already-good intent, via a tooling limitation neither the Circuit Engineer's design document review nor the PCB Engineer's own PCB README narrative caught. Independently confirmed via TI's own SLMA002/SLMA004 PowerPAD app notes that the exposed pad requires a thermal via array (not just a bare copper pour) to conduct heat through to an internal/bottom ground layer; U5's footprint (already ASSUMPTION-labeled by the PCB Engineer) was independently confirmed this cycle to have no such via array. Combined effect: even if a future fix adds the missing schematic pin and nets it, the footprint itself would still under-perform the datasheet's RθJA=36.1°C/W figure, which is a via-stitched-pad condition, not a bare-pad one -- a compounding, not merely sequential, defect | (1) Add pin 25 (EP) to U5's custom KiCad symbol and wire it to GND, matching U6's own symbol convention; (2) independently verify/replace U5's footprint with one that includes a proper thermal via array under the pad sized per TI's SLMA002/SLMA004 guidance (typical: a grid of 0.3-0.33mm vias, tented or filled, beneath the pad); (3) re-run DRC/ERC and the pad/net audit to confirm pin 25 is netted and via-stitched, not merely visually present | hardware-reviewer | [human sets] | 2026-09-14 | 2026-09-02 | Distinct in root cause from ISS-032/033/034 below (all three of which share one PCB-script net-assignment bug) -- U5's defect originates one stage earlier, in the schematic symbol definition itself, and is compounded by an independent footprint gap. Found via direct comparison of U5's and U6's custom symbol pin counts/definitions in `generate_schematic.py`, cross-checked against each part's real pin count from its own datasheet Pin Configuration table. `hardware/power-budget.md`'s U5 thermal-margin narrative (already a tracked non-blocking gap per the design doc's own §16 item 21, re: precise worst-case wattage) is further undermined by this finding: the qualitative margin claim assumes normal PowerPAD thermal conduction, which this pad, as currently implemented, cannot achieve. (3) Independently re-verified: kicad-cli sch erc stays clean (0 errors, same 1 pre-existing benign warning); a standalone pad/net audit confirms all 20 physical pad-25 members (2 large lands + 18 thermal vias) now share one single correct GND net, not merely visually present; DRC re-run x3 stays within the established baseline band (384/367/374 violations, 0 unconnected items), no regression introduced by the footprint swap. The exact EP numeric dimension remains an ASSUMPTION -- the primary datasheet's own mechanical package-outline drawing is a vector image, still not text-extractable by this session's tooling, same disclosed limitation as DS-MTR-052 -- but this no longer blocks a genuine, via-stitched thermal connection, which was this finding's core concern.. **INDEPENDENTLY CONFIRMED RESOLVED 2026-09-02 (Hardware Reviewer, Cycle 8):** The author's self-declared RESOLVED holds up under independent scrutiny. | ISS-032 | HIGH | RESOLVED | J4's disclosed ASSUMPTION-labeled tip/sleeve pin-mapping, and the design's own stated claim that D2's series reverse-polarity diode "fails safe even if J4's pin-mapping is swapped", is unsound for an internal (schematic/footprint) pin-mapping error, as opposed to an external (user plugs in a reverse-polarity adapter) one -- if J4's ASSUMPTION is wrong, GND (shared with J1/J2/J3) could be hijacked to +9-13V with no fusing in the return leg | J4 (barrel jack connector); D2 (series reverse-polarity Schottky diode); GND net (shared across J1 USB-C/J2/J3/J4) | `datasheets/samesky_pj-102ah_rev1-05.md` (primary datasheet for J4's part -- independently re-confirmed this cycle that the mechanical pin-mapping diagram needed to fully resolve the tip/sleeve ASSUMPTION could not be captured this session either, a real unresolved gap, not merely re-cited); DS-PROT-005 (STPS3L60 Schottky rectifier ratings, used to confirm D2's protection mechanism itself is otherwise correctly sized for the external-reversal case it does protect against) | D2 is wired in series with only ONE of J4's two conductors (per the schematic's own topology, independently traced via the exported netlist) -- this correctly blocks current if an EXTERNAL power adapter is plugged in with reversed tip/sleeve polarity (the scenario D2 was designed for). It provides no protection at all against an INTERNAL error: if J4's own footprint pin-mapping ASSUMPTION (which physical pad is tip vs. sleeve) is actually wrong, the conductor the design believes is GND could in fact be internally wired to the sleeve/tip carrying +9-13V, and since that same GND net is shared across J1/J2/J3, this would place +9-13V directly onto the USB-C/logic-domain ground reference with no series fusing or blocking element anywhere in that specific path -- a materially different and more severe failure mode than the one D2 was actually designed to catch, that the safety narrative does not distinguish from the case it does cover | Independently confirm J4's real tip/sleeve pin-mapping against the connector manufacturer's mechanical drawing (not just the electrical datasheet) before board fabrication, or add a second, independent protection element (e.g. a low-value fuse or polyfuse in the GND return leg itself, not just the series diode in the supply leg) so that an internal pin-mapping error cannot hijack the shared GND reference even in the worst case; explicitly narrow the safety-argument claim in the design documentation to state which specific failure mode (external vs. internal) D2 actually covers | hardware-reviewer | [human sets] | 2026-09-14 | 2026-09-02 | Independently traced the exact topology via the freshly-exported netlist (not the schematic's own prose description) before concluding the safety argument's scope gap -- confirmed D2's series placement and the shared GND net membership directly. This finding concerns the ORIGINAL Rev 3 J4/D2 topology (schematic-stage, unchanged by this session's PCB layout work) -- newly surfaced by this cycle's independent re-derivation of the design's own stated safety claim, not previously logged. Cross-ref: `validation/design-review.md` Hardware Reviewer -- Cycle 6. **DESIGN-LEVEL FIX ATTEMPTED 2026-09-02 (PCB Engineer, at explicit Chief Engineer direction -- "take a real crack at this as a Circuit Engineer-level design question before treating this as an escalation"):** Added F2, a second Littelfuse 30R500UF PTC resettable fuse (identical MPN/rating to F1, DS-PROT-006/032/033), in series between J4's sleeve/GND pin and the shared ground net (see `hardware/schematic/bench-imu-01-design.md` section 7.5.9 for full reasoning and the corrected/narrowed D2 safety-argument text). This makes the worst case safe *regardless* of which physical pin is actually tip vs. sleeve, without resolving the underlying pin-mapping ASSUMPTION: in normal (correctly-mapped) operation F2 passes ordinary ground-return current well within its 5A hold rating (same margin F1 already relies on for the <=3A worst case); in the fault case (mapping reversed), F2 sees the full supply rail driving into the low-impedance ground plane, trips well below its 40A fault rating, and then strongly current-limits in its tripped state -- converting an indefinite, unprotected hijack into a brief, self-limiting, automatically-resettable event. Self-verified this session (not a substitute for independent review, disclosed as such): kicad-cli sch erc stays clean (0 errors); kicad-cli sch export netlist confirms J4 pin1 no longer directly shares the GND net, instead routing through a new /J4_GND_RAW net into F2, with F2 pin2 the only path onward to GND; a standalone pad/net audit confirms F2's own pad connectivity; kicad-cli pcb drc re-run x3+ stays within the established baseline band (369-380 violations across repeated runs, 0 unconnected items) after placing F2 and routing it in. One genuine, unrelated bug found and fixed in the course of this work: the custom Fuse footprint's own Value text field contained the full descriptive footprint filename as a ~41mm-wide visible string, inflating pcbnew's reported footprint bounding box (~41x21mm vs. the real ~14x14mm courtyard) and causing a false courtyard-overlap/PTH-inside-courtyard/silk-over-copper reading against F2's first placement attempt -- fixed by hiding the Value field, matching standard KiCad convention (also benefits F1, pre-existing, not previously triggered since nothing sat close enough to it before). **Status intentionally left OPEN, not self-declared RESOLVED**: this project's own convention is that the fixing author does not mark a finding RESOLVED -- the independent reviewer does, after their own re-verification (confirmed this cycle by checking how ISS-026 was actually closed: the Rev 6 fix commit itself left status untouched; only the subsequent Cycle-5 reviewer commit set RESOLVED). This is also a genuine electrical-topology change, normally Circuit-Engineer scope rather than PCB Engineer -- made here only because the human Chief Engineer coordinating this cross-branch task explicitly directed it as a deliberate, disclosed exception, not a silent role-boundary violation. Recommend a focused Hardware Reviewer re-verification of this specific fix next, mirroring the Cycle 5/7 precedent.. **INDEPENDENTLY REVIEWED 2026-09-02 (Hardware Reviewer, Cycle 8) -- REMAINS OPEN, fix NOT accepted.** The schematic half of the F2 fix is confirmed correct and is worth keeping: own `kicad-cli sch erc` = 0 errors (1 pre-existing benign `lib_symbol_mismatch` warning on TLV75533PDBV); own `kicad-cli sch export netlist`, parsed with a from-scratch parser, confirms `/J4_GND_RAW` has EXACTLY 2 nodes (J4 pin1, F2 pin1) and that J4 is NOT a member of `/GND` (46 nodes, includes F2 pin2) -- so F2 is topologically the sole path and 100% of J4 sleeve current traverses it, with no schematic-level bypass. DS-PROT-006's specs (Ihold=5.00A, Itrip=10.00A, Vmax=30Vdc, Imax=40A, Rmin=0.010ohm, R1max=0.050ohm, derating to 2.60A@85C) are cited CORRECTLY by the commit. F2 is genuinely placed AND routed on the board (own `pcbnew` audit: F2 at (83.0,8.0), pad1=`J4_GND_RAW`, pad2=`GND`), DRC over 4 independent runs gives 369/375/369/372 violations (inside the claimed 369-380 band) with `unconnected_items`=0 and `schematic_parity`=0 on every run. **BUT the fix as implemented on this board is defective and is not accepted.** The fix's own new net `J4_GND_RAW` -- which now carries the ENTIRE motor ground-return current -- is routed as a single 0.250mm-wide, 22.000mm-long F.Cu track (plus one degenerate zero-length segment), while every sibling net in the same current loop (`VM_MOTOR_RAW`/`VM_MOTOR_F1`/`VM_MOTOR`/`U5_VCC`/`MOTOR_PHASE_U/V/W`) is 1.0mm. Loading the PRE-fix board (`c436ca9`) with the same audit shows J4 pad1 was previously reached by THREE 1.0mm GND tracks (17.77+2.92+2.83mm) -- so this fix reduced the ground-return copper cross-section by roughly 12x. Root cause: `generate_pcb.py`'s width rule is `WIDTH_HIGH_CURRENT if net in HIGH_CURRENT_NETS or net == "GND"`, and the renamed net matches neither, falling through to `WIDTH_SIGNAL`=0.25mm. This is logged separately as **ISS-039 (HIGH)**. It matters for THIS finding specifically because ISS-032 is a SAFETY finding: F2 was chosen as a *resettable* device so a fault is self-limiting and recoverable, but it now sits in series with a 22mm 10-mil trace that is the weakest element in the leg -- in the 5-10A window F2 never trips while the trace sits far past its thermal limit, and at >=10A the trace's fusing time and the PTC's multi-second trip time are the same order of magnitude. The likely real outcome of the fault F2 was added to survive is that the PCB trace burns open. Closing a safety finding on a topology the physical implementation defeats would be exactly the fake-Design-Complete this project forbids. Two further gaps found and logged separately: **ISS-040 (MEDIUM)** -- F2's series resistance IS in the U5 UVLO loop (the design doc's dismissal is electrically wrong; true margin ~0.26V, not the ~0.32V still stated in 6 doc locations); **ISS-041 (LOW)** -- F2's new sec 7.5.9 text omits the three honesty caveats F1's own adjacent text carries. Also recorded (not a finding): F2's protective merit is real but narrower than claimed -- with an isolated supply and J4 alone, the reversed-mapping fault is blocked by D2's reverse bias and F2 never trips; the hazard needs a second externally-referenced ground path, which J1/J2/J3 do provide. Process assessment: the role-boundary exception was disclosed in FOUR places and the author correctly left this OPEN rather than self-closing -- handled appropriately, and the reason this review could catch ISS-039 before closure. Expected to close on the next cycle jointly with ISS-039. Cross-ref: `validation/design-review.md` Hardware Reviewer -- Cycle 8, Item 1 **Cycle 8 follow-up re-verification (2026-09-02, hardware-reviewer): RESOLVED.** The single blocking objection recorded at Cycle 8 was ISS-039 -- that F2's own series leg was routed at 0.250mm, making the trace, not the PTC, the weakest element in the protected path and defeating the resettable-protection intent. That is now corrected and independently confirmed (see ISS-039). Re-verified this cycle that nothing else drifted: (1) Fresh ERC export: 0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated warning (U3 TLV75533PDBV lib_symbol_mismatch). (2) Fresh netlist export, parsed with my own reader: `/J4_GND_RAW` has EXACTLY 2 nodes, J4 pin1 and F2 pin1; `/GND` has 46 nodes and includes F2 pin2; J4 pin1 is NOT a direct member of `/GND`. F2 remains topologically the sole path from J4's sleeve to the shared ground -- no schematic-level bypass. J4 pin2 remains unconnected, F1 remains J4 pin3 -> `/VM_MOTOR_RAW` -> `/VM_MOTOR_F1` -> D2 pin2. (3) Layout path re-audited end to end: J4 pad1 (83.00,30.00) -> 22.000mm 1.0mm F.Cu track -> F2 pad1 (83.00,8.00); F2 pad2 (93.20,8.00) -> 10.000mm 1.0mm In1.Cu track, so the continuation past F2 is also 1.0mm and there is no downstream width bottleneck. F2's pads are THT (1.20mm drill, all copper layers). U5 pad-25 is still 20 members all on GND and the board-wide multi-pad net-consistency audit still returns 0 mismatches. (4) Adversarially checked whether the wider copper created a NEW proximity problem: zero DRC violations on either the old or the new board name `J4_GND_RAW`, and zero violations have any item anywhere in the x 81-85 / y 6-32 corridor the trace occupies. The four remaining narrow 0.250mm GND segments sit 60-84mm away in the J1/USB region and are pre-existing and previously disclosed. (5) The recorded reasoning nuance from Cycle 8 STANDS and is not blocking: with a genuinely isolated supply and J4 as the only external ground reference, a reversed J4 mapping is blocked by D2's reverse bias and F2 never trips; F2's protection is actually engaged when a second externally-referenced ground path exists (J1 USB-C, J2 UART, J3 SWD), which is the realistic bench configuration. The design doc's sec 7.5.9 now carries the narrowed claim plus the 5-10A unprotected-window and Ihold-derating caveats (ISS-041), so the documented claim matches the real protective envelope. Verdict: the fix bounds a previously indefinite, completely unprotected ground-hijack into a current-limited, self-resetting fault event in the configurations where it can occur, F2 is now the weakest element in its own leg as a protective device must be, and no bypass path exists as drawn. Closing. |

Status intentionally left OPEN. **FURTHER ANALYSIS 2026-09-02 (PCB Engineer, continued-iteration round per Chief Engineer disposition):** Went beyond bulk category counts to a real net-pair and item-type breakdown, using `kicad-cli pcb drc --format json` parsed programmatically (not manually sampled) -- concrete, additional root-causing, not yet full individual triage of every violation, but a real step beyond "366/380 unattributed": (1) **shorting_items severity check**: of 71 current shorting_items, 61% involve GND and would very likely represent a genuine electrical fault if fabricated as-is, not a benign artifact -- e.g. 8 instances of VBUS_5V-vs-GND, 5 of 3V3-vs-GND, 3 of U5_V3P3-vs-GND, 3 of the I2C1 bus shorted to GND or to itself (SCL-vs-SDA), 2 of a motor phase or the fused/protected VM_MOTOR rail vs GND -- this confirms the finding's own HIGH severity classification is well-supported by evidence, not merely a suspicion of unexamined bulk violations. (2) **shorting_items item-type root-cause split**: 48% are via-vs-track conflicts, specifically a through-via (any net other than GND) physically punching through copper on In1.Cu at a point where a GND track also runs on that same dedicated layer -- root cause: `generate_pcb.py`'s vias are ordinary THROUGH vias (span all copper layers including In1.Cu) and GND is routed as discrete tracks rather than a filled zone with automatic net-based anti-pad clearance (`ZONE_FILLER.Fill()` still segfaults in this environment, see `hardware/pcb/README.md`) -- a filled GND zone would have automatically kept a clearance moat around every other net's via at fill time; discrete tracks provide no such automatic protection. 41% are track-vs-pad conflicts, of which about a quarter are the same-component pin-cluster bridging tracks (Step A) running close to an unrelated pin's pad at U5's 0.65mm lead pitch -- a genuinely hard fine-pitch-routing constraint, not an oversight -- with the remainder being general routing-density close-passes consistent with this project's own already-disclosed routing-technique plateau. Only ~11% are simple track-vs-track (the class the 6 already-tried techniques targeted). **tracks_crossing (78 current instances) is 100% track-vs-track**, i.e. squarely the class those 6 techniques already addressed but could not fully eliminate without a real autorouter. **Recommended next step, now root-cause-specific rather than generic**: the via-vs-track class (48% of shorting_items) is the most tractable next fix -- either get `ZONE_FILLER.Fill()` working (a genuine tooling-environment fix, out of this session's own reach) so GND becomes a real filled zone with automatic via clearance, or add a targeted post-routing pass that checks every non-GND via's proximity to existing GND track segments and relocates any conflicting via -- NOT attempted this session: modifying already-placed via positions risks silently disconnecting the track segments that terminate at them, a real regression risk this session judged not worth taking without a wider time budget to verify thoroughly, especially for a safety/connectivity-adjacent change this late in an already long session. Documented here as a well-scoped recommendation for whoever picks this up next, not silently deferred. **REAL FIX ATTEMPTED AND HONESTLY REVERTED 2026-09-02 (PCB Engineer, per Chief Engineer's explicit "push this to real closure if you can" direction):** Three concrete pieces of work, none of which land as a false claim of closure. (1) **Triage conclusion for shorting_items**: DRC's shorting_items check only ever fires between two DIFFERENT nets, so there is no "same-net, therefore harmless" subset to find -- every named-net-vs-named-net pair (VBUS_5V-vs-GND, 3V3-vs-GND, a motor phase or the fused VM_MOTOR rail vs GND, the I2C1 bus shorted to itself or to GND, etc.) represents a genuine would-be short if fabricated exactly as drawn; only the small fraction involving a truly <no net> mechanical/unused pad (~8% of the set sampled) is benign. This confirms the finding's own HIGH severity classification is not paranoia -- there is no large hidden-harmless bucket to subtract, most of this category needs an actual fix, not just a disposition. (2) **Rule-tuning check (per the finding's own suggestion)**: independently queried the board's actual solder-mask design-rule settings (m_SolderMaskExpansion=0.05mm, m_SolderMaskMinWidth=0.1mm, m_SolderMaskToCopperClearance=0.05mm) -- these are already standard, fab-house-typical values, not needlessly strict ones a rule change could safely loosen; doing so would hide the problem rather than fix it and push toward exotic/expensive fab tolerances. Not a viable systemic fix. (3) **A genuine code fix was attempted for the largest identified root cause (via-through-GND-layer punctures, ~48% of a sampled shorting_items set)**: added a post-routing pass to generate_pcb.py that reroutes GND track segments (never moves other nets' already-placed vias, to avoid repeating the ISS-038 class of regression) around any non-GND via found within clearance, iterating to a proper fixed point with explicit oscillation detection (a genuinely tight cluster of short bridge segments near J1's USB-C connector, one zero-length, could not cleanly clear within 2 detour attempts -- reported as an honest residual rather than looped on indefinitely or silently under-fixed). **Independently DRC-verified across 3 runs before deciding whether to keep it**: the fix is a genuine but MIXED result, not a clear win -- tracks_crossing improved meaningfully (~78 to ~53-55, a real ~30% reduction) but shorting_items got WORSE (~71-76 to ~84-85), because a detour routed clear of the one via it targeted sometimes passes closer to a different net's copper than the original straight segment did -- the fix has no visibility into the rest of the board's copper, only the specific via it's avoiding. Given shorting_items is the category this finding itself flags as closest to an actual short (most safety-relevant), trading a tracks_crossing improvement for a shorting_items regression is not an honest net improvement -- **reverted, not shipped** (git checkout back to the last committed state, re-verified DRC returns to the established ~369-380 baseline band). **The precise tooling wall, stated plainly**: a fully correct fix needs either (a) pcbnew.ZONE_FILLER.Fill() working, so GND becomes a real filled zone with KiCad's own automatic net-based via-clearance/anti-pad logic (confirmed segfaults in this environment, unchanged from prior sessions, see hardware/pcb/README.md), or (b) a detour algorithm that checks a candidate reroute path against the ENTIRE board's existing copper (every other net, not just the one via being avoided) before committing to it -- which is no longer a targeted post-processing fix but starts to approximate building a real autorouter from scratch, genuinely beyond what this session judged safe to attempt and fully verify in the time remaining. Filling that one unrelated zone also didn't move the DRC needle either way (noise-band). Status remains OPEN (not RESOLVED, not ACCEPTED-RISK) -- this is the correct, honest status for a genuinely deferred decision, and `tools/check_open_issues.py` correctly continues to fail the Design Complete Gate on this exact item. **REAL FIX FOR OPTION (a) IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-02 (PCB Engineer, per Kyosuke's explicit go-ahead to resume real engineering work on this exact finding):** Re-isolated the `ZONE_FILLER.Fill()` segfault precisely rather than treating it as flaky -- it is NOT flaky: `Fill()` against this exact GND-on-In1.Cu zone/board segfaults 100% of the time (3/3, two different placements tested) when called in the SAME process that incrementally built the board via the pcbnew Python API, and succeeds 100% of the time (15+ calls) against a board freshly reloaded via `pcbnew.LoadBoard()` from an already-saved file -- reconciling the Chief Engineer's own successful re-test (which loaded a saved file) with the original segfault reports (always in-process) without needing "non-deterministic". Implemented in `generate_pcb.py` itself (regeneratable, not a hand patch): build the board with GND's normal discrete In1.Cu tracks as always (guarantees a valid connected board no matter what follows), save, reload fresh, attempt the fill there, and if it succeeds (`zone.IsFilled()` confirmed) remove the now-redundant discrete GND tracks on In1.Cu (vias stay) and re-save; any failure falls back to the original safe behavior automatically. Verified with real DRC data, apples-to-apples against a freshly-regenerated CONTROL board (unmodified script) to isolate the fix's own effect from ordinary regeneration noise (a real but small effect, confirmed separately): shorting_items (the category this finding itself flags as closest to an actual short) drops from 74-75 (control, 5 runs) to 61-62 (fixed, 5 runs) -- a genuine ~17% reduction with zero overlap across all 10 runs. GND's own involvement in tracks_crossing is flat-to-better (15/69 control vs 12/82 fixed); the increase in that category is entirely among OTHER net pairs, with DRC-engine violation-report suppression at shared physical locations (masking one violation type when a co-located one of a different type existed) as the leading, not fully confirmed, explanation. 0 unconnected items in every one of all 10 runs both before and after -- GND connectivity fully preserved (every GND pad/via in this design's actual footprint set independently confirmed to fall inside the zone's poured area). This is a genuine, right-priority trade-off (improves the more safety-relevant category), unlike the earlier reverted detour attempt -- but does NOT fully close this finding: ~365-380 total violations remain, the "every violation individually triaged" resolution bar is not yet met. Full technical detail in `hardware/pcb/README.md`. Crash-safety design independently confirmed sound: the code saves a valid, fully-connected board BEFORE the reload+fill step, so a hypothetical future segfault during that later step would still leave a valid file already on disk; also confirmed `except Exception` can only ever catch a genuine Python-level exception, not an actual segfault, and the fix's own narrative does not claim otherwise. **Verdict: CONFIRMED** -- the claimed ~17% `shorting_items` improvement and the 0-unconnected safety property both hold up under independent re-verification, with no other category showing an undisclosed regression.
