plausibly related to the same 0.65mm fine-pitch package-proximity characteristic already documented for U5/U6 elsewhere in this triage (U5's own adjacent I2C pins), just manifesting here as a via-vs-inner-layer-track instance rather than the track-vs-pad/via-vs-track-outer-layer shape that category otherwise covers. Not attempted this cycle, either instance: In2.Cu carries multiple different nets (unlike In1.Cu's single dedicated GND net), so the same "declare one zone, fill it" technique doesn't directly generalize -- a real per-via clearance/reroute approach would be needed, which is exactly the class of fix (per-via, per-net, whole-board-aware) the earlier reverted detour attempt already demonstrated produces an unreliable mixed result on this board's geometry; flagged as a specific, well-scoped candidate for the same future dedicated session Kyosuke already authorized for option (b), not attempted ad-hoc here. **The remaining 54 (89%) fall into categories already understood and disclosed as genuinely hard, autorouter-class constraints, not fresh findings**: 28 via-vs-track conflicts and 22 track-vs-pad conflicts on the OUTER (F.Cu/B.Cu) layers -- the same "no real routing engine" limitation this design has disclosed since its first DRC pass -- of which 14 specifically match the already-documented U5/U6 0.65mm-fine-pitch-package-proximity pattern (verified geometrically: violation position within 8mm of U5's or U6's placed center); plus 4 pure track-vs-track crossings. **Net effect of this pass**: confirms (not merely repeats) that the bulk of what remains is the disclosed autorouter-class gap, narrows the "needs a real answer" subset from 58 (all non-benign) down to a specific, named 4-item pocket with an identified mechanism and a scoped future approach, and does not claim to have individually resolved any of the 58 -- still correctly OPEN, still correctly failing the gate. **WHOLE-BOARD-AWARE REROUTE FIX 2026-09-02 (PCB Engineer, per Kyosuke's explicit "build real whole-board-aware routing capability now" go-ahead, resuming full engineering effort on option (b)):** Built a whole-board-aware collision checker using `pcbnew`'s own `SHAPE.Collide`/`SEG.Collide` geometry primitives (KiCad's own DRC engine, not a hand-rolled approximation) that checks every candidate reroute path against the board's ENTIRE other-net copper set before accepting it -- the specific gap that made the earlier reverted detour attempt produce a mixed/regressive result. Of the 4 named `via_vs_inner_layer_copper` targets from the prior triage: found whole-board-clear detours for all 3 J1-area instances (GND-through-via-vs-VBUS_5V-In2.Cu-track); directly confirmed (geometric distance measurement, not assumption) the 4th (I2C1_SDA-vs-I2C1_SCL near U5) is genuinely intractable -- the two vias sit at U5's own 0.65mm pin pitch with only a 0.05mm physical gap between copper edges, below any usable clearance regardless of technique. Also found 1 additional outer-layer fix (a VM_MOTOR track near J4) via an automated batch search; 4 further iterations at search widths up to 9mm found 0 more tractable fixes -- a genuine plateau, not a search-parameter limit, confirming the remaining ~54 outer-layer violations are the already-disclosed autorouter/fine-pitch-proximity-class gap (a substantial fraction confirmed via direct geometric check to cluster near U5/U6). Did not attempt a U5/U6 placement change this round -- U5's placement was already the subject of ISS-031's own thermal-via fix, and shifting it risks reopening that fix without a wider verification budget than this round allows; a scoped candidate for a future session, not silently declined, per Kyosuke's own explicit "doesn't need to reach zero violations" framing. All 4 fixes integrated into `generate_pcb.py` via a new `REROUTE_OVERRIDE` list + `_apply_reroute_overrides()` (matches by net+position, not UUID/object identity, since every script re-run creates fresh objects; loud warning and skip, never silent no-op or partial apply, if a source segment goes stale). **Verified via real, repeated DRC** (3 fresh `kicad-cli pcb drc` runs before vs. after): shorting_items 61-62 -> 56-58 (genuine reduction, directly confirmed -- all 3 J1-area instances absent from fresh output, U5-area instance still present as expected); tracks_crossing 81-82 -> 71-72; clearance 11 -> 16-17 and hole_clearance 3 -> 6 (apparent increases) were each INDIVIDUALLY re-verified, not inferred from the count delta -- enumerated all 19 unique pairs across the 3 "after" runs, matched each to the identical two objects (net+position+length, all three required, in both the current board and a pristine pre-round baseline) and computed clearance via `GetEffectiveShape()` on both sides (width-aware, matching DRC's own method): all 19 show numerically IDENTICAL clearance in both boards (e.g. GND-vs-CC1-via at exactly 0.150mm both before and after) -- conclusively pre-existing, DRC-masking-related (the same phenomenon already documented for the zone-fill fix's tracks_crossing increase), not new problems. Process note disclosed rather than omitted: an earlier same-day comparison (matching across two separately-regenerated boards by position+net alone, computing clearance via a bare SEG that silently omitted the track's own half-width) produced a false "6 genuinely new" result -- caught and corrected before committing by re-deriving every pair with a fixed, symmetric, width-aware method against the real committed boards directly. Net effect: 4 of the original targeted/discovered fixes applied and DRC-confirmed with zero new violations in any category; ~355-370 total violations remain; sent for independent Hardware Reviewer verification before any partial-closure claim; Status remains correctly OPEN -- individually-triaged-and-closed is still not met (4th via_vs_inner_layer_copper instance confirmed intractable; ~54 outer-layer violations remain a disclosed, not-attempted-further, autorouter/placement-class gap). Full technical detail in `hardware/pcb/README.md`. **HARDWARE REVIEWER CYCLE 9 INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION 2026-09-02:** Confirmed via own tooling (not by trusting the report above) -- re-ran DRC 3x independently, checked new copper against all other-net objects on the board (not a sample), independently re-measured the U5-area 0.05mm via-to-via gap two ways (arithmetic + Collide()-binary-search), and independently re-derived all 22 unique clearance/hole_clearance pairs (not just a 4-5 item sample) against the pre-round baseline (`19ffb16`): all 22/22 numerically identical before/after, confirming pre-existing. **Verdict: PASS** for this round's claims -- all 3 J1-area fixes and the 4th target's intractability independently reconfirmed, zero undisclosed regression in any category, `unconnected_items`/`schematic_parity`=0 throughout. Found 3 LOW-severity, non-substantive issues, none affecting the core engineering conclusions: (1) this file's/README's "14 of ~54 within 8mm" figure does not exactly reproduce (reviewer obtains 11/6/16 depending on counting rule, never exactly 14 -- corrected above); (2) README's claim the U5-area conflict is "now reported as 3 separate DRC entries rather than 1" is inaccurate -- the identical 4-entry signature (1 clearance + 3 shorting_items) already existed in the pre-round baseline, not new (corrected above); (3) one incidental, fully pre-existing/benign `shorting_items` instance (`VM_MOTOR_RAW` vs J4 pin 2, already explained by open ISS-017's unpopulated 3rd terminal) doesn't cleanly fit the prior round's own "3+4+54=61" bucket accounting -- a triage-completeness gap, not a defect. Does not change ISS-036's Status (correctly remains OPEN) or grant any partial-closure disposition. Spatial check: 59% (125/211) within 15mm of U5/U6 (matches the fine-pitch-proximity pattern already documented), but 41% (86/211) spread board-wide -- meaning even a perfect U5/U6 placement fix could only address 59% of this one category; combined with U5/U6's own neighboring components sitting 11-20mm apart (not tightly packed), this is quantified evidence the router's own lack of routing-congestion awareness, not simple placement, is the more fundamental limitation. **U5/U6 placement redesign considered and declined again, now with data rather than only the prior round's risk argument.** Uniquely identified 206/211 violations' specific track objects and ran the same whole-board-aware collision search (checking every candidate detour against the board's ENTIRE other-net copper): found only 2 tractable fixes (~1% yield) -- matching the ~2% yield already found for the 54-item outer-layer shorting_items sweep, now confirmed via a SECOND independent 206-item test. This is reproducible evidence local single-track detour search has a real, low-single-digit-percent ceiling for this board, not a fluke. Both fixes (3V3 near U1, VM_MOTOR near J4/M1) added to REROUTE_OVERRIDE (5 total now), verified via 5 fresh DRC runs: shorting_items improved further (56-58 -> 49-50); both targeted conflicts confirmed absent from fresh output (checked against exact pre-fix path signature, not aggregate counts); the tracks_crossing increase (72-73->81) and hole_clearance decrease (5-6->3) were individually verified (84 unique pairs checked, including pad-based items via direct pad lookup) -- all 84 confirmed pre-existing/unchanged, 0 genuinely new, re-confirming the same 3 J1/U2-area hole_clearance items Hardware Reviewer Cycle 9 already verified pre-existing. 0 unconnected items across all 5 runs. Net: 2 more real fixes (7 total across both rounds); ~350-365 total violations remain; ISS-036 remains correctly OPEN -- resolution bar still not met. | ISS-037 | MEDIUM | RESOLVED | `hardware/pcb/README.md`'s own IPC-2221 citation for its 1.0mm motor-domain trace-width choice is internally inconsistent (the cited 30-35mil figure for a 3A/10C-rise target is only correct for roughly a 20C rise, not 10C), and independently-measured narrow ~0.25mm "stub" segments exist near component pads on 5 motor-domain nets -- a routing-script artifact -- though the 1.0mm trace width itself is independently confirmed adequate once DRV10983's correct continuous (not peak/fault) current rating is used as the sizing basis | Motor-domain copper (VM_MOTOR and related power nets on `bench-imu-01.kicad_pcb`) | DS-MTR-054 (DRV10983 Section 7.3 Recommended Operating Conditions -- continuous current basis); DS-MTR-058 (Section 8.3.2.3 Overcurrent Protection -- confirms the 3-4A `IOC_limit` figure the PCB README's own citation appears to have used is a peak/fault threshold, not the part's continuous operating current) | The PCB README derives its 30-35mil-per-1oz-copper-per-10C-rise figure from a standard IPC-2221 external-layer trace-width chart, but the specific 30-35mil value it cites actually corresponds closer to a ~20C temperature rise at 3A on that same chart, not the stated 10C -- an internal citation/arithmetic inconsistency independently re-derived this cycle directly from the IPC-2221 chart values, not merely re-read from the README's own claim. Separately, the "3A" current figure itself is DRV10983's OCP/fault threshold (DS-MTR-058), not its continuous operating current (~2A per DS-MTR-054/056 and this design's own power-budget nominal figure) -- using the correct continuous-current basis instead, the board's actual 1.0mm (~39mil) trace width independently computes to roughly 28% current-carrying margin over a proper 10C-rise IPC-2221 target, which is adequate despite the citation error, so this finding is about the flawed derivation/citation and the separate narrow-stub geometry, not a live undersized-trace defect on the primary basis. Independently measured, via direct `.kicad_pcb` track-segment geometry inspection, narrow ~0.25mm-wide short segments immediately adjacent to component pads on 5 distinct motor-domain nets -- consistent with a routing-script artifact (likely a pad-escape or via-transition segment inheriting a default trace width rather than the intended power-net width) rather than an intentional design choice | Correct the PCB README's IPC-2221 citation to accurately reflect the 10C-rise target's actual corresponding trace width, and explicitly state the continuous-vs-fault-current distinction used for sizing; re-run PCB generation with the routing script's stub-segment behavior fixed so all segments of the 5 affected motor-domain nets carry the intended full-width copper, not a narrower default near pads | hardware-reviewer | [human sets] | 2026-09-14 | 2026-09-02 | Independently re-derived the IPC-2221 chart values directly rather than accepting the README's own citation at face value, per this review's independence mandate; the underlying 1.0mm trace width choice itself independently passes on the corrected (continuous-current) basis, which is why this is logged as a MEDIUM documentation/derivation-quality and minor-geometry finding rather than a HIGH undersized-copper finding. Cross-ref: `validation/design-review.md` Hardware Reviewer -- Cycle 6. **RESOLVED 2026-09-02 (PCB Engineer, continued-iteration round per Chief Engineer disposition):** Both halves fixed. (1) Citation math: independently re-derived via the IPC-2221 external-layer formula (I=0.048*dT^0.44*A^0.725, 1oz copper) and cross-checked against a published reference table -- confirmed the finding's claim exactly: ~30-35mil is the width for 3A at a ~20C rise (calculated 35.5mil), not a 10C rise (calculated 54mil); separately confirmed DRV10983's correct sizing basis is its 2A CONTINUOUS rating (DS-MTR-034), not its 3A start-up/locked-rotor rating (DS-MTR-056) -- at 2A/10C the required width is ~31mil (0.79mm), so the design's actual 1.0mm (~39mil) trace IS adequate with real margin, just not for the reason/citation originally stated. README corrected to state the right basis. (2) Stub segments: root-caused to generate_pcb.py's same-component pin-cluster bridging step, which hard-coded a flat 0.25mm width for every bridge regardless of net class -- affected exactly the 5 nets the finding identified (MOTOR_PHASE_U/V/W, U5_VCC, VM_MOTOR); fixed by reusing the same net-current-class width selection the main routing step already uses. Independently re-verified via a standalone per-net trace-width audit script: all 5 nets now uniformly 1.0mm end to end (a VM_MOTOR_F1 '0.6mm' item found during the audit is a via's diameter, not a track, confirmed by object type -- not a stub-width defect, no fix needed there). DRC re-run x3 after the fix: 374/372/379 violations, 0 unconnected -- within the same baseline band, no regression.. **INDEPENDENTLY CONFIRMED RESOLVED 2026-09-02 (Hardware Reviewer, Cycle 8):** Verified for its own stated scope by this reviewer's own per-net trace-width sweep of the committed `.kicad_pcb` (written fresh, not taken from the commit message): `MOTOR_PHASE_U` (3 tracks), `MOTOR_PHASE_V` (3), `MOTOR_PHASE_W` (3), `U5_VCC` (8) and `VM_MOTOR` (10) are ALL uniformly 1.0mm -- no narrow stubs remain on any of the five. `VM_MOTOR_RAW` is also 1.0mm; `VM_MOTOR_F1` is a 1.0mm track plus a 0.6mm via, and the 0.6mm is confirmed by item type to be a via DIAMETER, not a trace stub, consistent with the PCB Engineer's own note. The citation half also checks out: this reviewer independently re-derived 54mil @3A/10C, 35.3mil @3A/20C and 30.8mil (0.78mm) @2A/10C from the IPC-2221 external-layer formula, reproducing the corrected README figures exactly (this derivation was also used as the method check for ISS-039's numbers). **Caveat recorded, not a reason to reopen:** the fix corrected the five NAMED nets but not the underlying net-classification mechanism in `generate_pcb.py`, which recurred immediately on the very next net the project added -- see **ISS-039**. ISS-037 stays RESOLVED because it is honest to close a finding on its own stated scope, but ISS-039's remedy should address the classification mechanism rather than just adding one more string to `HIGH_CURRENT_NETS`. Layout evidence is this cycle's own independently-run `kicad-cli pcb drc --format json` output and an independent `pcbnew` geometry audit, not a datasheet citation | `generate_pcb.py`'s ISS-033/034/035 fix adds, for every extra physical pad sharing a pad number, a straight point-to-point F.Cu segment from the first pad to that pad, with no obstacle/DRC awareness and with the layer hard-coded to F.Cu regardless of the target pad's actual layer set. Then re-run DRC and confirm the U6 exposed-pad region returns to zero different-net same-layer crossings, and that `unconnected_items` and the whole-board pad/net audit both stay clean | hardware-reviewer | [human sets] | 2026-09-02 | 2026-09-02 | Found during Cycle 7's focused re-verification of the e63e62c fix round (`validation/design-review.md` -- Hardware Reviewer -- Cycle 7). ISS-033/034/035 themselves are correctly RESOLVED: every one of U6's 17 pad-"21" pads, J1's 4 pad-"SH" pads and SW1's 2+2 pads does now carry one single correct net, independently confirmed by this reviewer's own `pcbnew` audit. This row is strictly about the NEW copper the fix added, not about the net assignment it fixed. Distinct from ISS-036 (the aggregate 366-violation DRC-closure gate): these 12 crossings are newly introduced by this fix round, are individually root-caused, and have a targeted fix that does not require solving ISS-036 as a whole -- logging them separately prevents them disappearing into an aggregate count. Note that the fix round's own ISS-036 disclosure characterises the increase as "additional clearance/hole_clearance violations in that immediate vicinity"; that is honest in intent but factually incomplete -- clearance rose only +2 and hole_clearance +4, while the dominant new class is +11-12 different-net same-layer crossings, which is a short-risk class rather than a manufacturing-margin class **RESOLVED 2026-09-02 (PCB Engineer, same-day follow-up fix after Hardware Reviewer Cycle 7 logged this finding):** Root cause of the redundant/harmful bridges was that `generate_pcb.py`'s multi-pad bridging step picked `matching_pads[0]` (an arbitrary small 0.6x0.6mm thermal-via pad, footprint-file order) as the reference/hub point and pairwise-tested each OTHER small via's bounding box against that arbitrary via -- neighbouring 0.6mm vias spaced 1.3mm apart never geometrically intersect each other directly, so every one was (wrongly) judged to need its own bridge, even though all 15 sit inside the much larger shared F.Cu/B.Cu exposed-pad land pads that are ALSO members of the same pad-number group. Fixed by selecting the hub pad as the LARGEST-bounding-box-area member of the group (`max(matching_pads, key=lambda p: p.GetBoundingBox().GetArea())`) instead of index 0 -- this correctly identifies the land pad as the hub for U6's PowerPAD group (all 16 other members' bboxes are contained within the land's, so 0 bridges are now drawn there) while leaving J1's shield (3 bridges) and SW1's terminal pads (1 bridge each) correctly still bridged, since those groups have no single large land pad. Verified: an independent bounding-box audit confirms U6 pad-21 now needs 0 bridges (was 16); `kicad-cli pcb drc --format json`, net-pair-parsed, confirms GND-vs-U6_ILIM crossings dropped 12 -> 0 board-wide. **Correction (Hardware Reviewer Cycle 7 follow-up, commit f55d8f7, independently re-verified rather than trusted at face value):** this session's own first re-check mis-attributed one remaining nearby violation as a GND-vs-U6_ILIM crossing; the reviewer's own independent DRC re-run correctly identified it as a GND-vs-DIR shorting_items violation instead (still pre-existing/unrelated to bridging, confirmed present in the Cycle 6 baseline 84db343 -- the underlying substance was right, the specific net-pair claim was not) -- it belongs to ISS-036, not here. Re-verified across a fresh 4-run DRC re-check; the standalone pad/net audit (ISS-033/034/035) re-confirms U6 pad 21 (17 pads), J1 SH (4 pads), and SW1 pads 1/2 (2+2) still each share one correct net with no mismatch. Whole-board violation count is now within the same band as the pre-fix baseline (this session's own re-runs: 365/373/371/365; the reviewer's independent re-runs: 379/379/370/368, against a 377 baseline re-run) across repeated runs -- **corrected (Cycle 7 follow-up): back within the original ~370-377 pre-any-fix baseline band, not strictly at-or-below it as first claimed** (the non-determinism spans both sides of the baseline for both this session's and the reviewer's own runs) -- so this round closes with a net IMPROVEMENT over baseline, not merely a wash, while fixing the underlying net-assignment defects (ISS-033/034/035) that baseline never actually had correct to begin with. unconnected_items remains 0 across all runs. |
| ISS-039 | HIGH | RESOLVED | The ISS-032 fix's own new net `J4_GND_RAW` -- which carries the entire motor-domain ground-return current -- is routed as a single 0.250mm-wide, 22.000mm-long F.Cu track (plus one degenerate zero-length segment), while every sibling net in the same current loop is 1.0mm; the pre-fix board reached the same J4 pad through three 1.0mm GND tracks, so the fix cut the ground-return copper cross-section by roughly 12x | `J4_GND_RAW` net between J4 pad1 (83.00,30.00) and F2 pad1 (83.00,8.00) (`hardware/pcb/bench-imu-01/bench-imu-01.kicad_pcb`; root cause in `hardware/pcb/bench-imu-01/generate_pcb.py` lines 246-254 and the width-selection expressions at 560-563 / 643-646) | DS-MTR-034 (DRV10983 2A continuous rating -- the sizing basis ISS-037's own resolution established as correct), DS-MTR-056 (3A start-up/locked-rotor, this design's stated <=3A worst case), DS-PROT-006 (Littelfuse 30R500U/UF: Ihold=5.00A, Itrip=10.00A, Imax=40A -- establishes the fault-current range F2 is meant to act over, and therefore what the series trace must survive). Layout evidence is this reviewer's own `pcbnew` per-net trace-width audit of the committed board plus the same audit run against the pre-fix `c436ca9` board, not a datasheet citation; the thermal figures are this reviewer's own IPC-2221 hand-derivation | `generate_pcb.py` selects width as `WIDTH_HIGH_CURRENT if net_name in HIGH_CURRENT_NETS or net_name == "GND" else WIDTH_POWER if net_name in POWER_NETS else WIDTH_SIGNAL`. `HIGH_CURRENT_NETS` is a hard-coded string set `{VM_MOTOR_RAW, VM_MOTOR_F1, VM_MOTOR, U5_VCC, MOTOR_PHASE_U/V/W}`. Before the ISS-032 fix, J4 pin1 was literally on net `GND` and therefore picked up 1.0mm via the `== "GND"` special case. The fix renamed that leg to `J4_GND_RAW`, which matches neither the set nor the literal, so it silently fell through to `WIDTH_SIGNAL` = 0.25mm. Two distinct consequences. (1) NORMAL OPERATION: by the design's own IPC-2221 formula (I=0.048*dT^0.44*A^0.725, 1oz external, A=13.56 mil^2), 0.25mm carries 0.88A at a 10C rise; required width at the 2A continuous basis / 10C is 0.79mm, so the net is ~3.1x undersized. Implied rise is ~65C at 2A and ~164C at the stated <=3A worst case (the latter far outside chart validity, i.e. indicative of gross undersizing). Method was validated by reproducing ISS-037's own published numbers exactly (54mil @3A/10C, 35.3mil @3A/20C, 30.8mil @2A/10C). (2) IT DEFEATS THE ISS-032 REMEDY: F2 was chosen as a *resettable* PTC so a fault is self-limiting and recoverable, but it is now in series with a 22mm 10-mil trace that is the weakest element in the leg. In the 5-10A window F2 does not trip at all (Itrip=10A) while the trace sits far past its thermal limit; at >=10A the trace's fusing time and the PTC's multi-second trip time are the same order of magnitude. The likely real outcome of the very fault F2 was added to survive is that the PCB trace burns open -- a non-resettable, potentially damaging failure -- rather than a clean PTC trip. Stated explicitly as an order-of-magnitude engineering argument, NOT a sourced number: this repository holds no time-to-trip curve for the part (`grep -ci "time.to.trip" datasheets/evidence-log.md` = 0) and no trace-fusing data, so no precise crossover current or time is claimed | Widen `J4_GND_RAW` to the same 1.0mm high-current class as every other net in the motor current loop, and delete the degenerate zero-length segment at (83.00,8.00). Prefer fixing the CLASSIFICATION MECHANISM rather than just adding one more string: derive a net's width class from its role (e.g. any net galvanically in the J4/F1/F2/D2/U5/U6 motor power-and-return loop) so the next net the project adds cannot silently inherit `WIDTH_SIGNAL` the same way -- this is the second time this exact defect class has appeared (see ISS-037, whose fix corrected the five named nets but left the mechanism intact). Then re-run `kicad-cli pcb drc` and re-confirm `unconnected_items` = 0, and re-run the per-net width audit to confirm `J4_GND_RAW` reports a single 1.0mm width. Once verified, ISS-032 and ISS-039 should close together | hardware-reviewer | [human sets] | 2026-09-02 | 2026-09-02 | Found by an independently written `pcbnew` per-net trace-width audit of the committed `.kicad_pcb`, then root-caused by reading `generate_pcb.py` directly and confirmed as a REGRESSION by running the identical audit against the pre-fix `c436ca9` board (where J4 pad1 was reached by three 1.0mm GND tracks). Severity reasoning, stated openly: CRITICAL was considered, because at the design's own <=3A worst case the computed rise is damage-level, but nominal running current is ~1.05A (~15C rise, survivable) and the 3A figure is a start-up transient rather than a continuous condition, so `docs/architecture.md` sec 7.1's CRITICAL test ("will fail or cause damage/hazard under normal/expected operating conditions") is not met with certainty. HIGH is the honest call ("likely malfunction or reliability failure under realistic conditions/corners"). If the Hardware Lead judges the 2A CONTINUOUS rating rather than the ~1.05A nominal to be the binding normal condition, escalation to CRITICAL is defensible and this reviewer would not argue against it. Separately recorded and deliberately NOT logged as a finding: the board carries 4 other 0.25mm `GND` segments (near J1, and at (60.00,62.50)); all four are byte-identical in the pre-fix board, i.e. pre-existing and outside this focused cycle's scope. Cross-ref: `validation/design-review.md` Hardware Reviewer -- Cycle 8, Item 1g **FIXED 2026-09-02 (PCB Engineer, same-day follow-up after this finding was logged):** Root cause confirmed exactly as the reviewer found -- `generate_pcb.py`'s `HIGH_CURRENT_NETS` string set did not include the new `J4_GND_RAW` net (introduced by the ISS-032 fix), so it silently fell through to `WIDTH_SIGNAL` (0.25mm) instead of `WIDTH_HIGH_CURRENT` (1.0mm). Fixed by adding `J4_GND_RAW` to `HIGH_CURRENT_NETS`. Independently re-verified: a direct pcbnew query confirms both J4_GND_RAW track segments are now 1.0mm wide (was 0.25mm); DRC re-run x3 stays within the established baseline band (369/370/372 violations, 0 unconnected). Status intentionally left as set by the reviewer (OPEN) -- not self-marked RESOLVED, pending the reviewer's own re-verification of this specific fix, per this project's author/reviewer separation convention. **Cycle 8 follow-up re-verification (2026-09-02, hardware-reviewer): RESOLVED.** Independently re-derived, not accepted on the author's attestation. (1) Own `pcbnew` per-net width query of the committed `.kicad_pcb` returns a per-net width set of exactly {1.0} for `J4_GND_RAW` -- BOTH items (the 22.000mm F.Cu run (83.00,30.00)->(83.00,8.00) and the zero-length segment at (83.00,8.00)) are now 1.000mm. (2) Checked for a hidden side effect of the `HIGH_CURRENT_NETS` membership change as directed: `generate_pcb.py` references that set at exactly two sites (lines 580 bridge-step and 663 main MST routing), and both are the SAME width-selection ternary; layer assignment keys off representative-point bounding boxes and via sizing is independent, so set membership can affect trace width and nothing else. No hidden semantics. (3) Wrote a from-scratch geometry-diff of the pre-fix board (b54d5f7) against the current one: 227 vs 227 tracks, 42 vs 42 vias, and the geometry-only signature (net, layer, endpoints) is IDENTICAL with zero items only-in-old and zero only-in-new; the ONLY differences on shared geometry are exactly two width changes, both `J4_GND_RAW` 0.250mm -> 1.000mm. The fix is surgical -- no track moved, no via moved, no other net's width changed. (4) Thermal check re-derived independently from IPC-2221 (I = 0.048 * dT^0.44 * A^0.725, 1oz external = 1.378mil): 1.0mm = 39.37mil, A = 54.25mil^2, giving approx. 2.39A at a 10C rise, approx. 6.7C rise at the 2A continuous basis (DS-MTR-034) and approx. 16.7C at the 3A start-up transient (DS-MTR-056) -- comfortably within the design's own accepted sizing basis, versus 0.88A/10C and approx. 65C at 2A for the old 0.250mm trace. (5) DRC run 8x on the current board and 8x on the pre-fix board in the same session for a true baseline: unconnected_items = 0 and schematic_parity = 0 in all 16 runs; totals 368-388 (median 373.5) new vs 370-380 (median 373.5) old -- statistically unchanged. (6) The Cycle 8 sub-recommendation to delete the degenerate zero-length segment is WITHDRAWN: a board-wide audit shows 16 such segments across many nets at several widths, a benign systemic artifact of the MST/bridge routing algorithm that DRC does not flag, not a J4-specific defect. Closing on the primary defect (the 12x copper reduction), which is fully corrected. |
| ISS-040 | MEDIUM | RESOLVED | F2's series resistance IS in the U5 UVLO series loop, contrary to the explicit claim in `generate_schematic.py`'s comment and `bench-imu-01-design.md` sec 7.5.9 that it is "not a new term in any existing voltage-drop calculation"; the design's tracked ~0.32V UVLO margin is therefore stale and the true figure is ~0.26V, still stated as ~0.32V in six document locations | F2 (Littelfuse 30R500UF) in J4's ground-return leg, and the sec 7.5.2 3S-only UVLO margin analysis it feeds (`hardware/schematic/bench-imu-01-design.md` lines 163, 1496, 1909, 3122, 3870-3871, 4166) | DS-PROT-006 (30R500U/UF Rmin(initial)=0.010ohm, R1max(post-trip)=0.050ohm -- the basis for the design's own 0.02ohm assumed in-circuit resistance), DS-PROT-032/033 (30R500U == 30R500UF electrically), DS-MTR-080 (the V_VCC(U5)-to-RPM derivation the commit invokes when dismissing the effect), and the DRV10983 `VUVLO_R` max = 8.0V figure already cited in sec 7.5.2 | U5's VCC is measured RELATIVE TO BOARD GND. After the ISS-032 fix, board GND is displaced from J4's sleeve by F2's I*R drop, so F2 sits in the same series loop as F1 and D2 -- the dismissal reasoning ("the GND reference is not part of the VM_MOTOR series-drop budget at DS-MTR-080") confuses the *reference* node with the *loop*. Using the design's own method and own constants (0.02ohm assumed in-circuit PTC resistance x 3A = 0.06V, exactly as already applied to F1 at design-doc lines 1491-1498): documented margin = 9.0V - 0.62V (D2 VF max) - 0.06V (F1) = 8.32V vs `VUVLO_R` max 8.0V => ~0.32V; true margin with F2 = 8.26V => ~0.26V. That is a further ~19% erosion of a tracked margin on this design's own BINDING 3S-only constraint. At DS-MTR-080's actual operating point (no-load, 0.3A) the effect genuinely is negligible -- 3mV, ~6 RPM against a stated 25,060-25,280 band -- so the CONCLUSION "negligible" is right there, but the stated REASON is wrong and the effect is NOT negligible at the 3A UVLO corner where the margin is actually evaluated | Correct the claim in both `generate_schematic.py`'s inline comment and `bench-imu-01-design.md` sec 7.5.9 to state that F2 IS in the U5 supply series loop, and update the sec 7.5.2 UVLO margin derivation to include F2's drop. Propagate the corrected ~0.26V figure to all six locations that currently state ~0.32V (lines 163, 1496, 1909, 3122, 3870-3871, 4166). Keep the DS-MTR-080 no-load observation but restate it accurately: negligible AT THAT OPERATING POINT, not negligible in principle. | ISS-054 | LOW | RESOLVED | Footprint **emission order** in `generate_pcb.py`'s output is not stable run-to-run, so a byte/hash comparison of a **regenerated** board or of its exported gerbers reports differences on a provably unchanged design — ISS-050 established this hazard for `kicad-cli pcb drc` but never extended it to naive byte comparison, nor named this mechanism | Verification method for `hardware/pcb/bench-imu-01/bench-imu-01.kicad_pcb` and its exported gerbers (not the board geometry itself, which is reproducible) | N/A — tooling-behaviour finding. Authority: 6 independent `generate_pcb.py` runs (KiCad 10.0.1 bundled Python 3.9, macOS/arm64) plus a 27-file `kicad-cli pcb export gerbers` comparison; committed board sha `068ad9df917afeb5392b724a5b606f7c1d8e2c66` re-verified unchanged before and after the series | Not electrical — **a measurement-validity trap that can manufacture a phantom regression**, the same class as ISS-050 but reached by a different route. Two consecutive regenerations from byte-identical inputs emit the same 54 footprints in a **different order**: 41 of 54 positions differ between two fresh runs, and 47 of 54 differ against the committed board, while the footprint *multiset* is identical in all three. Because the order in which footprints are emitted determines the order in which aperture shapes are first used, KiCad assigns different D-code numbers to the same shapes, so a naive content hash of the exported gerbers flags **9 of 27** exported files as differing — including **6 of the 11 fabrication gerber layers** (`B_Cu`, `F_Cu`, `In1_Cu`, `In2_Cu`, `F_Silkscreen`, `F_Paste`) — even though **all 27 are identical under aperture-aware fingerprinting**. A future cycle that regenerated the board and reached for the obvious instrument (`diff`/`shasum` on the gerbers, or on the `.kicad_pcb` itself) would therefore see a large apparent change on an unchanged design | Record that **byte/hash comparison is not a valid instrument for comparing a regenerated board, or its exported gerbers, against the committed one** — extend ISS-050's existing "DRC is not valid for this" guidance to cover naive byte comparison, and use only the instruments ISS-050 already endorses and that this audit independently re-confirmed: footprint/track/via multisets, pad and net counts, the zone `filled_polygon` vertex set, and aperture-aware gerber fingerprinting (resolve each D-code to its shape/parameters before comparing, so pure renumbering does not register) | rubber-duck | N/A (LOW — does not gate Design Complete per `docs/architecture.md` §8) | 2026-09-03 | 2026-09-03 | Raised by the independent overnight audit of PR #34 (already merged) and fixed in the same PR that raised it, per the ISS-046/047 and ISS-050/051 precedent. **This finding strengthens ISS-050 rather than contradicting it**: re-running ISS-050's own endorsed instruments against a freshly regenerated board independently reproduced its central claim — tracks `175`/`175` and vias `42`/`42` multiset-identical, pads `264`/`264`, footprint set identical at `54`, and all 27 exported files identical under aperture-aware fingerprinting, with `In1_Cu` carrying `3871` coordinate lines and exactly one `G36`/`G37` region on both sides. The GND zone was specifically tested for the same instability and is **fully deterministic**: `filled_polygon` = `3769` vertices on the committed board and on 3 further independent runs. **This is also explicitly not a contradiction of ECO-045/PR #34's "all 11 fabrication gerber layers re-exported byte-identical to the committed zip"** — that re-export was taken from the committed board *file*, which carries a fixed footprint order by construction, whereas this finding concerns re-*generating* the board; both statements are true and describe different operations. **Withdrawn intermediate claim, disclosed rather than dropped** (ISS-051 precedent): this audit first measured an apparent "GND zone fill varies 3785–3812 vertices run-to-run" and then refuted it by its own follow-up — the search substring `\t(zone` also matches `\t\t(zone_connect` inside footprint bodies, so the count had in fact been taken from a mid-file footprint through end-of-file and was tracking footprint *order*, not the zone. Correctly anchored to the top-level zone, the fill is the constant `3769` above. No board, gerber, net, footprint, requirement, dimension, BOM or firmware content is changed by this row. |

| MISS-023 | HIGH | ACCEPTED-RISK | REQ-407(b) pinch-point/rotating-overhang hazard is not assessed anywhere in the Rev 4 free-rotation mechanical package, despite a concrete, independently-derived physical mechanism: the rotating assembly's own farthest perimeter point sweeps ≈115.9mm from the bearing axis while the stationary stand plate's outer radius is only 60.0mm | Rev 4 free-rotation assembly as a whole (rotating `base()`+`bmount_flange()` perimeter vs. stationary `stand_plate()`), `hardware/mechanical/bench-imu-01-enclosure.scad` | `requirements/requirements.md` REQ-407(b) (line 200) and REQ-408 (line 201, both Must-priority/safety-critical, same HITL gate class as REQ-403); `bom/component-selection.md`'s Free-Rotation Support Mechanism section (explicit deferral of this hazard class to this exact phase); `.scad` lines 596-597 (`fw_cx`/`fw_cy`), 636-641 (`assembled_envelope_y_north`=168.4mm), 830 (`stand_plate_or`=60.0mm), 861-869 (Rev 4 Z-stack) — all independently traced this cycle, not previously computed anywhere in the Rev 4 deliverable | A finger, hand, loose cable, or other object resting/reaching on the desk anywhere in the 60.0-115.9mm annular band from the bearing axis is struck or pinched between the rotating overhang and the desk surface as the platform sweeps past, at a linear speed scaling with the commanded platform rate (up to multiple m/s at REQ-012's own disclosed upper rate bound, ~1170 deg/s) — no guard, warning, or mitigation of any kind currently designed or acknowledged | Perform the REQ-407(b)-mandated pinch-point assessment explicitly as its own dimensional-spec subsection (not folded into the tip-over analysis); select and document a mitigation: a physical guard/skirt reducing accessible clearance in the swept zone, an enlarged stand-plate footprint closer to the true swept radius, and/or an explicit human-attended-operation warning naming this specific hazard zone. Owner: Mechanical Lead | mechanical-reviewer | HIGH: [human sets] | 2026-09-14 | 2026-09-15 | Surfaced during Independent Mechanical Review of the Rev 4 free-rotation support mechanism addition (see `validation/design-review.md`, "Mechanical Reviewer — Cycle 5 (Independent Review of Rev 4 Free-Rotation Support Mechanism)," 2026-09-14, Finding 1). Independently derived this cycle via direct `.scad` geometry tracing — not previously flagged anywhere in the Mechanical Lead's own §18.7 self-check, §18.10 UNKNOWNs table, or §18.11 handoff notes, and not a re-statement of a Mechanical-Lead-disclosed caveat. Classified HIGH, not CRITICAL, per the same reasoning `docs/architecture.md` §7.1 and this project's own MISS-016 precedent already establish: the underlying physical precondition (an unguarded overhang sweeping well beyond the stand's footprint) is confirmed real, but whether it actually causes injury at intended/typical operating speeds has never been assessed either way — an absent required safety assessment, not a confirmed-bad outcome under normal conditions. Blocks a clean PASS for this cycle; ties to the same HITL gate class as REQ-403/MISS-016 per REQ-407/REQ-408's own explicit text, and `bom/component-selection.md` already explicitly deferred this exact hazard class to this exact phase, so no legitimate further deferral point exists. **Cycle 6 (2026-09-15) re-review of the Mechanical Lead's Rev 4.1 `pinch_guard()` mitigation — status remains OPEN/HIGH, not RESOLVED, not ACCEPTED-RISK:** independently re-derived the true max swept radius from raw `.scad` geometry as 126.424mm (confirming the Mechanical Lead's own correction of this Reviewer's prior Cycle 5 115.9mm figure — an X-offset this Reviewer itself missed in Cycle 5); independently re-rendered and confirmed `pinch_guard()` (annulus 60.0-115.0mm radius, 14.9mm height, split into 4 printable quadrants) is genuinely non-overlapping with the rotating envelope (empty boolean intersection, both `show_mode`s re-rendered) and flush/zero-volume against `stand_plate()` at r=60.0mm, via direct OpenSCAD+trimesh re-render, not trusted from prose; independently recomputed the coverage math (hazard annulus area = pi*(126.424^2-60.0^2) = 38,902.4mm^2, guarded area to 115.0mm = 30,238.3mm^2, ratio = 77.73%, matching the claimed 77.7% exactly); independently rendered `pinch_guard()`'s own mass (570.558g vs. the claimed ~570.6g, exact match) and reconciled the full system mass to the claimed ~1173.4g total to within 0.05g. Endorses, and independently extends, the Mechanical Lead's own honest self-assessment ("partially closed...I do not believe this should be marked fully RESOLVED as-is") for two additional adversarial reasons the Lead's own write-up discloses as facts but does not weigh into the disposition itself: (1) the residual 11.4mm gap sits at the OUTER radius band (115.0-126.424mm) — the highest tangential-velocity, most directly/horizontally-reachable portion of the hazard (no reach-over-barrier maneuver required, unlike the guarded 60-115mm band), meaning the 77.7%-by-area figure likely overstates real-world risk reduction since the guard's protection is binary-by-footprint, not proportional to area; (2) `pinch_guard()` is explicitly self-disclosed (dimensional-spec §18.12.7, §18.16) as unfastened/unkeyed to `stand_plate()` — a free, desk-resting ring with no positive locating feature — meaning even the nominally-guarded 77.7% zone's real-world protection could degrade from bumps/handling over the service life, a risk this Reviewer judges under-weighted in the closure framing though honestly disclosed as a fact by the Mechanical Lead. Net: real, verified, substantial mitigation (77.7% of the hazard band is now physically guarded, confirmed by rendering, not merely asserted) but not an adequate disposition for a HIGH REQ-407(b)/REQ-408 safety finding via a paper-and-procedure-only closure of the remainder — remains OPEN/HIGH pending either (a) further geometric closure (extending `pinch_guard_or` toward 126.424mm and/or adding a positive guard-retention/locating feature) or (b) a human Chief Engineer ACCEPTED-RISK disposition with named sign-off, which this Reviewer cannot supply. See `validation/design-review.md`, "Mechanical Reviewer — Cycle 6," 2026-09-15, Finding 1. **ACCEPTED-RISK — human Chief Engineer sign-off recorded 2026-09-15** (received directly from Kyosuke, relayed via the cross-session HITL channel — see `validation/change-log.md` ECO-031 for the corresponding entry): `pinch_guard()`'s current design (77.7%-by-area coverage of the true 126.424mm-radius hazard band, 60.0–115.0mm radius, 4 printable quadrants) is approved as a **defense-in-depth mitigation** — explicitly on the basis of a real, disclosed, partial risk reduction, **not** on a claim that the residual 11.4mm outer-band gap has been closed or that the unfastened/driftable guard's real-world durability has been proven. Both of Cycle 6's own independently-derived aggravating factors (the residual gap sits at the highest-velocity, most-directly-reachable outer band; the guard's own lack of a positive retention/locating feature) were explicitly weighed as part of this decision, not disputed or minimized. This mirrors the exact disposition class already established at MISS-016/REQ-403 (Rev 3, ECO-024/025): a real, non-overclaimed partial mitigation, accepted by named human sign-off, not a silent drop or a self-declared full closure. No further geometric closure (extending `pinch_guard_or` toward 126.424mm, adding a retention feature) is required this cycle — that remains available as a future refinement if this disposition is ever revisited, not a blocker now. |

Disposition: RESOLVED for the REQ-407(c)/REQ-113 Must-scope this finding was actually opened against. Two distinct consequences. (a) `.github/instructions/validation.instructions.md` requires that a HIGH finding may become `ACCEPTED-RISK` only with a named human Chief Engineer sign-off, written rationale, **and date** — MISS-016 is exactly such a disposition, so a 12-day-wrong date means a *required schema field on a safety disposition* is factually false, and that false value is now being copied forward into Rev 5 (REQ-409). (b) The `SLA Target` column is only meaningful relative to `Opened`; a finding "opened" in the future has an uncomputable and always-negative age, so SLA tracking is silently inoperative for 47 of 87 rows. Previously uncaught across 27 prior cycles. **Instrument-error guard, stated explicitly because two earlier cycles on PR #39 had to publicly retract load-bearing claims for exactly this class of mistake:** (1) *Timezone* — a JST/UTC confusion (the cycle-25 error) cannot produce this, because the largest gap is **+14 days**, not ±9 hours, and because `Resolved < Opened` is a row-internal contradiction that holds under any single timezone. (2) *Parser* — this finding was first derived with `awk -F'\|'`, which splits naively and **does not understand Markdown's `\|` escape**. That instrument produced a **false positive** which is hereby recorded and withdrawn rather than filed: it reported ISS-046 as a malformed 15-column row, when ISS-046 is in fact correct 13-column Markdown whose Failure Mechanism cell legitimately contains an escaped regex (`` `^(X\|D0\|G3[67])` ``). Re-derived with a Markdown-aware splitter (`re.split(r'(?<!\\)\|', ...)`), **0 of 87 rows are malformed** and the column order is intact. The date finding above **survives** the corrected instrument unchanged (47 future, 8 inverted) and is what is actually being filed. **CI-gate integrity was separately and empirically tested, and the gate is sound:** temporarily forcing ISS-046 to `CRITICAL`/`OPEN` was correctly detected by `tools/check_open_issues.py`, and the file was restored byte-identical afterwards — so no claim is made here that the gate mis-parses anything. This row is MEDIUM and therefore does **not** itself change `hardware-gate` status, which remains red solely on MISS-034. **Corrected in-place by cycle 29 (independent audit of this PR, pre-merge — no merged record is being rewritten).** Cycle 29 re-derived every claim above and confirmed them exactly (87 rows; 47 future-`Opened`; max `2026-09-17`; precisely ISS-030…ISS-037 inverted; the full skew table ISS-014 +5 / ISS-030 +12 / MISS-016 +12 / MISS-030 +14 / MISS-033 +14 / MISS-034 +0; MISS-016 confirmed `HIGH`/`ACCEPTED-RISK` with its sign-off note itself reading `2026-09-13` against introducing commit `33859a3` dated `2026-09-01`). **But cycle 28's corroborating claim that `validation/change-log.md` was correct was false, and it was false through exactly the sampling error this row warns about** — it checked only the three newest ECO rows, which sit in the same recently self-corrected window as MISS-034, and generalized. Reading the whole column shows 21 of 47 ECO rows dated in the future, verified two independent ways (Markdown-row pickaxe, and a full `git log -p` walk for the first commit adding each row; author date == committer date throughout, so this is not a rebase artifact). The `Title`, `Component`, `Evidence` and `Recommended Fix` cells above were corrected accordingly; **no other row, and no `Severity`, `Status`, disposition or date value anywhere in this file, was touched** — in particular MISS-016's dates remain untouched and human-reserved |
